*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address read series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x24)
* One address in the auto-incrementing range returns SLVERR/DECERR mid-series
* Good beats before the fault return reference data at their incremented addresses
* SERIES_CTRL status matches the injected fault; bridge recovers afterward

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Pre-load the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with reference data across the range and configure a non-OKAY response at one address.
. For each beat i: load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and address base{plus}i*stride; Update-DR.
. Shift SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_INCR (IR=0x24) once to launch the read and wait for the AXI-Lite read handshake; shift it again to capture the returned beat.
. After the faulting beat, capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify good beats return the reference data at their incremented addresses, the SERIES_CTRL status after the failing beat carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code with that beat's read data treated as invalid, and the address progression matches the expected increment.
. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing read beat carries the injected error code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats match the expected incrementing-address references, the failing beat reports the injected error, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-423-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_write_test]]
===== 4.23 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_write_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP no-increment write error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) write series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x25)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat; AXI-Lite address remains fixed for every beat
* SERIES_CTRL status identifies the failing beat while good beats commit correctly to the fixed address
* Bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return a non-OKAY response on a selected beat at the fixed target address.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the fixed address; Update-DR.
. Shift each beat through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x25); every Update-DR issues a write at the fixed address.
. After each beat's AXI-Lite write handshake, check the memory at the fixed address; after the faulting beat, capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code, good beats committed their data to the fixed address, the faulting beat left the memory unchanged, and the AXI-Lite address never incremented on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`.
. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) write to a clean address; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_wr_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing beat carries the injected error code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_wr_mem_chk` |Good beats commit to the fixed address; no address increment observed
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: The injected fault is reported on the failing beat, the address stays fixed, good beats commit correctly, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-424-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test]]
===== 4.24 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP no-increment read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) read series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x25)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat while the AXI-Lite address remains fixed
* Good beats return the reference value at the fixed address; failing beat marks invalid data
* SERIES_CTRL status matches the injected fault; bridge recovers afterward

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with a known value at the fixed address and a non-OKAY response on a selected read beat.
. For each beat: load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the fixed address; Update-DR.
. Shift SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_NO_INCR (IR=0x25) once to launch the read and wait for the AXI-Lite read handshake; shift it again to capture the returned beat.
. After the faulting beat, capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify good beats return the reference data, the SERIES_CTRL status after the failing beat carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code with that beat's read data treated as invalid, and the address never incremented.
. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_status_chk` |SERIES_CTRL status captured after the failing read beat carries the injected error code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the fixed-address reference; failing beat data is invalid
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats match the fixed-address reference, the failing beat reports the injected error, the address stays fixed, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-425-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test]]
===== 4.25 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP write with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address write series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26), with a fault mid-series
* Each with-error-status shift returns the previous beat's error status, so the fault surfaces on the shift after the faulting beat without a SERIES_CTRL capture per beat
* A trailing shift returns the last beat's status; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
* Good beats commit at their incremented addresses and the faulting beat leaves its address unchanged; bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return a non-OKAY response at one address within the incrementing range.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b10 (Write), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift each beat with increment 1 through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26) and run the series to completion; each Capture-DR returns the status bit of the previous beat.
. After the last beat, shift one trailing word (data 0, increment 0) to return the last beat's status bit; after the faulting beat, capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL and read its status field.
. Verify the status bit is 1 only on the shift after the faulting beat, good beats committed at their incremented addresses, the faulting beat left its address unchanged, and the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) write to a clean address; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_tdr_chk` |The status bit is 1 on the shift after the faulting beat and 0 on every other shift; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_mem_chk` |Good beats commit at correctly-incremented addresses
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing beat and error code, good beats commit, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-426-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test]]
===== 4.26 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP read with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address read series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge, carried by SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) and SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26), with a fault mid-series
* One SERIES_CTRL preload serves the whole read stream: the bridge advances the address per launching shift, and each shift returns the data and error status of the previous read
* Good beats return reference data with status bit 0; the shift returning the faulting read carries status bit 1 and SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
* Bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Pre-load the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with reference data and configure a non-OKAY response at one address in the incrementing range.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL (IR=0x23) with op=0b01 (Read), size, and the base address; Update-DR.
. Shift N words with increment 1 followed by one trailing word with increment 0 through SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_DATA_WITH_ERROR_STATUS (IR=0x26); shift k returns the data and status bit of read k-1.
. Capture SEP_OTP_AXI_SERIES_CTRL right after the faulting read's handshake and read its status field.
. Verify good beats returned the reference data at their incremented addresses, the status bit is 1 only for the faulting read, and the SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Issue a follow-up legal SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_tdr_chk` |The status bit is 1 only on the shift returning the faulting read; SERIES_CTRL status carries the injected SLVERR/DECERR code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: The with-error-status bit and the SERIES_CTRL status report the failing read beat and error code, good beats match references, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-427-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test]]
===== 4.27 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP error-path security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* SEP OTP bridge gating: `dbg_disable_i.sep_otp_jtag2axi = 1`
* When gated, SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) requests are suppressed: no AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`, even on error-path attempts, and no error-status update
* Two assert/release passes prove the gate is repeatable; each release restores normal and error-path operation

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With the AXI-Lite subordinate configured to return errors on the target region, assert `dbg_disable_i.sep_otp_jtag2axi = 1` to gate the bridge.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) and attempt an error-region write:
a. Verify no AXI-Lite transaction is issued on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` and no error-status latches.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify normal/error-path operation resumes and the error is reported as expected.
. Repeat steps 1–3 as a second pass of the same disable.
. Confirm that with all disables clear, ungated error-path accesses complete and report SLVERR/DECERR normally.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_gating_no_traffic_chk` |No AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` while gated
|`sep_otp_axi_err_gating_restore_chk` |Each release restores normal/error-path operation
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NOACT`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-AR`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-AW`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-W`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features. The scoreboard's blocked window (`CHK-AXI-BLOCKED`) fails on any monitored transaction across the gated attempt and its release, and the sentinel read-back is a plain assertion. The blocked path's positive control is the enabled baseline access of the same scenario.

*Pass Criteria*: The asserted disable forces BYPASS with no AXI-Lite traffic on the error path, and operation is fully restored after each release, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-428-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_aw_before_w_test]]
===== 4.28 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_aw_before_w_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: AW before W channel skew

*Test Coverage*:

* Maximum AW/W channel skew on the JTAG2AXI single-op write path with AW ready asserted while W ready is held low
* AXI-Lite `aw_valid_stable` / `w_valid_stable` and payload-stability assertions while ready is low
* The bench does not sample the FSM state sequence; the skewed write is judged by the AXI-Lite stability assertions and its OKAY completion

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive a JTAG2AXI single-op write through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Program the bridge address and data via the primary TAP and launch the write op.
b. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate to skew the channels: accept AW (ready high) while holding W ready low for an extended interval.
. While W ready is low, confirm AW/W valid and payload remain stable and do not deassert.
. While W ready is held (a stall sized to outlast the first status poll), observe the stall from the bridge's side: its AXI FSM sits on the write path (`CHK-J2A-STALL-FSM`, through the `dtp_tb_if` FSM mirror) and the first SINGLE_OP status capture reads Busy (`CHK-J2A-STALL-BUSY`).
. Release the W-channel backpressure and let the write drain.
. Verify the write completes with an OKAY response and read back the written location to confirm the value landed.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_bp_stall_chk` |While W ready is held the bridge FSM is observed on the write path and the first status poll reads Busy (`CHK-J2A-STALL-FSM`, `CHK-J2A-STALL-BUSY`)
|`j2a_bp_stable_chk` |AW/W valid and payload remain stable while the subordinate holds ready low
|`j2a_bp_status_chk` |Final JTAG2AXI completion status is OKAY and read-back data matches the written value
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features. Both realizations record `CHK-J2A-STALL-FSM` and `CHK-J2A-STALL-BUSY`. The bridge's own response evidence is its SINGLE_OP status (`check_status` SUCCESS, `CHK-SB-JTAG2AXI-STATUS`), corroborated by the responder burst count and the memory compare since OKAY is the all-zero encoding; `CHK-AXI-RESP` is the responder-side record.

*Pass Criteria*: The maximum-skew write holds the AXI-Lite stability assertions and completes correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-429-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_long_stall_test]]
===== 4.29 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_long_stall_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Long ready stalls

*Test Coverage*:

* Long subordinate ready stalls on AW, W, AR, and R channels exercised one channel at a time and combined
* Real success hits on the AXI-Lite `aw_valid_stable` / `w_valid_stable` / `ar_valid_stable` and corresponding payload `+*_stable+` assertions
* Reachable JTAG2AXI FSM dwell in `AXI_SEND_ADDR_W` / `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` (write) and the read-issue / wait states under prolonged stall
* No premature timeout, abort, or status corruption while a legal stall is in progress

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Set up stall stimulus on the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate:
a. Configure long, deterministic ready-low intervals on AW/W (write path) and AR/R (read path) that remain inside the architectural stall budget.
. Issue write traffic through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Launch a single-op write while AW/W ready are stalled for longer than the first status poll; observe valid/payload hold stable across the full stall window, the bridge FSM on the write path, and the first status capture reading Busy (`CHK-J2A-STALL-FSM`, `CHK-J2A-STALL-BUSY`).
b. Release the stall and confirm OKAY completion with the written word in memory.
. Issue read traffic through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Launch a single-op read while AR ready is stalled for longer than the first status poll; observe AR valid/address hold stable, the bridge FSM on the read path, and the first status capture reading Busy.
b. Release the stall and confirm the returned data matches the reference model.
. Verify the bridge does not declare a timeout or error during any legal stall and that each operation reaches its expected terminal status after release.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_long_stall_assert_chk` |AW/W/AR valid and payload `+*_stable+` assertions hold across the full stall window
|`j2a_long_stall_status_chk` |While each stall holds the bridge FSM is on the stalled path and the first status poll reads Busy (`CHK-J2A-STALL-FSM`, `CHK-J2A-STALL-BUSY`); no premature timeout/abort; each op reaches its expected terminal status after stall release
|`j2a_long_stall_data_chk` |Post-stall write-back and read-data compare against the reference model
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features. Both realizations record `CHK-J2A-STALL-FSM` and `CHK-J2A-STALL-BUSY`.

*Pass Criteria*: All AW/W/AR stable assertions receive real success hits under long stalls, every stall is observed from the bridge's side, no operation times out or aborts during a legal stall, and every read/write completes correctly after release with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-430-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_abort_at_data_w_test]]
===== 4.30 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_abort_at_data_w_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Reset abort around data phase

*Test Coverage*:

* A system reset (`rst_n_i`, the bridges' AXI-side reset) asserted while a SINGLE_OP write is held on the W channel of each bridge by a responder READY stall that is released only after the reset
* The bridge observed mid-flight before the reset through `dtp_tb_if`: its TCK-domain AXI FSM outside `AXI_IDLE` with the single-op pending flag set (the CDC accepts AW and W at once, so the FSM waits in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` while the responder holds the channel)
* Containment: the FSM back in `AXI_IDLE` within a bound after the reset, the CDC's TCK-side isolate-and-clear observed, and no write for the aborted operation reaching the subordinate
* Recovery: the SINGLE_OP status leaving `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and a legal write completing afterwards
* A bridge that stays in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` with status `BUSY_OR_FULL` after the reset fails `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each bridge: hold the responder's W channel with a READY stall that outlasts the scenario, record the target slot's prior word, and issue a SINGLE_OP write through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Step TCK in Run-Test/Idle while sampling the bridge FSM and pending flag through `dtp_tb_if` until the FSM leaves `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles (the FSM and the CDC's TCK side advance only while TCK runs); record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`.
. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag, pulse `rst_n_i` for one to three system cycles, then release the stall.
. Step TCK while sampling the FSM until it returns to `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`. Record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` from the sticky clear-seen flag and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` from the unchanged target slot.
. Poll the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `SUCCESS`, and on success issue a legal write to a clean slot with the memory compare.
. After all three bridges, fail the pass naming the bridges whose status stayed `BUSY_OR_FULL`.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT` |The FSM has left `AXI_IDLE` and the pending flag is set when the reset lands
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` |The FSM is back in `AXI_IDLE` within the bound after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` |The bridge's CDC ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` |The aborted operation's slot in the subordinate memory is unchanged
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` |The SINGLE_OP status leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and the follow-on legal write completes
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker, with `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`, `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` recorded by the sequence into the same finalization; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features. The credit of the mid-transaction containment is stated in <<dtp-vplan-signoff-j2a-dispositions>>.

*Pass Criteria*: On every bridge the reset lands with the FSM observed mid-flight, the FSM returns to `AXI_IDLE`, the CDC clear is observed, no write escapes, and the status recovers so the follow-on write completes, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-431-dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_narrow_reset_mid_xaction_test]]
===== 4.31 `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_narrow_reset_mid_xaction_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Narrow reset during transaction

*Test Coverage*:

* A system reset (`rst_n_i`, the bridges' AXI-side reset) asserted while a SINGLE_OP write is held on the AW channel of each bridge by a responder READY stall that is released only after the reset
* The bridge observed mid-flight before the reset through `dtp_tb_if`: its TCK-domain AXI FSM outside `AXI_IDLE` with the single-op pending flag set (the CDC accepts AW and W at once, so the FSM waits in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` while the responder holds the channel)
* Containment: the FSM back in `AXI_IDLE` within a bound after the reset, the CDC's TCK-side isolate-and-clear observed, and no write for the aborted operation reaching the subordinate
* Recovery: the SINGLE_OP status leaving `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and a legal write completing afterwards
* The single-cycle pulse exercises the `axi_cdc_clearable` reset controllers' isolate-and-clear on the TCK side; the sticky clear-seen flag in `dtp_tb_if` is the observation
* A bridge that stays in `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` with status `BUSY_OR_FULL` after the reset fails `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each bridge: hold the responder's AW channel with a READY stall that outlasts the scenario, record the target slot's prior word, and issue a SINGLE_OP write through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Step TCK in Run-Test/Idle while sampling the bridge FSM and pending flag through `dtp_tb_if` until the FSM leaves `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles (the FSM and the CDC's TCK side advance only while TCK runs); record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`.
. Clear the sticky CDC clear-seen flag, pulse `rst_n_i` for a single system cycle, then release the stall.
. Step TCK while sampling the FSM until it returns to `AXI_IDLE`, bounded in TCK cycles; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`. Record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` from the sticky clear-seen flag and `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` from the unchanged target slot.
. Poll the SINGLE_OP status up to eight times; record `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` against `SUCCESS`, and on success issue a legal write to a clean slot with the memory compare.
. After all three bridges, fail the pass naming the bridges whose status stayed `BUSY_OR_FULL`.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT` |The FSM has left `AXI_IDLE` and the pending flag is set when the reset lands
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM` |The FSM is back in `AXI_IDLE` within the bound after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` |The bridge's CDC ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear after the reset
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE` |The aborted operation's slot in the subordinate memory is unchanged
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` |The SINGLE_OP status leaves `BUSY_OR_FULL` within eight polls and the follow-on legal write completes
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker, with `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY`, `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` recorded by the sequence into the same finalization; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features. The credit of the mid-transaction containment is stated in <<dtp-vplan-signoff-j2a-dispositions>>.

*Pass Criteria*: On every bridge the reset lands with the FSM observed mid-flight, the FSM returns to `AXI_IDLE`, the CDC clear is observed, no write escapes, and the status recovers so the follow-on write completes, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-432-dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test]]
===== 4.32 `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Back-to-back CDC reset recovery

*Test Coverage*:

* Two system-reset pulses back to back across the TCK-to-ACLK domains on an idle bridge, the second inside the first's settle time
* The TCK-side isolate-and-clear of every bridge's CDC controller runs after the burst (the sticky `cdc_clear_seen` observable), and every pulse is counted (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`)
* The bench does not sample the FIFO and FSM idle after the burst; the settled state is judged by the post-recovery reads and writes completing with the expected status and data
* Post-recovery reads and writes complete correctly across all involved clock domains; a reset that lands on a bridge with a write in flight is the mid-flight abort scenarios' claim, not this one

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With the bridge idle, clear the sticky `cdc_clear_seen` flags.
. Apply two system-reset pulses back to back through `dtp_tb_if`, the second within the first's settle time, each counted by the tb_top reset-assertion counter.
. Step TCK and confirm every bridge's CDC controller ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear (`cdc_clear_seen`).
. Issue subsequent write and read operations and confirm each reaches its expected terminal status with correct data.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_clear_chk` |Every bridge's CDC controller ran its TCK-side isolate-and-clear after the reset burst (`CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR`), and both pulses were counted (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`)
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_recovery_chk` |Post-recovery reads/writes complete correctly with expected status and data
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features. Both realizations record `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` per bridge and `CHK-RESET-COUNT` per pulse.

*Pass Criteria*: Back-to-back resets on an idle bridge run every CDC controller's isolate-and-clear and are each counted, and subsequent reads and writes complete with the expected status and data on every bridge, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-433-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test]]
===== 4.33 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Bridge write DECERR handling, response injected by the responder, all three targets

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op write on each of the three bridges (SMC fabric AXI4, SMC OTP and SEP OTP AXI4-Lite) answered with DECERR by that target's `ocah_axi_vip` responder; the bridge ports leave the DTP straight into the responders, so the DTP boundary has no address decoder and no unmapped address, and an interconnect's decode-miss and default-slave behaviour is SoC-level verification
* The write status captures DECERR in op[1:0] and the scoreboard classifies the observed DECERR as the armed response
* The errored slot keeps its prior word (the responder drops an armed write beat)
* Bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent legal write on every target

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. On each target's responder, arm a one-shot DECERR write response at a beat-aligned slot and record the slot's word.
. Drive a JTAG2AXI single-op write to that slot on the bridge; poll the op-status until not Busy and confirm it reports DECERR.
. Confirm the slot's word is unchanged.
. Issue a follow-on legal write on the same bridge and confirm OKAY with the intent bytes landing in the responder memory.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_decerr_wr_status_chk` |The write status reports DECERR for the responder-injected response on every target, and `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` classifies the observed DECERR as armed
|`j2a_decerr_wr_mem_chk` |The errored slot's word is unchanged after the DECERR write
|`j2a_decerr_wr_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY and lands its bytes (`CHK-AXI-WMEM`)
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: Every bridge reports the injected DECERR, no memory side effect lands at the errored slot, and a follow-on legal write succeeds on every target, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-434-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test]]
===== 4.34 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Bridge read DECERR handling, response injected by the responder, all three targets

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI single-op read on each of the three bridges (SMC fabric AXI4, SMC OTP and SEP OTP AXI4-Lite) answered with DECERR by that target's `ocah_axi_vip` responder; the bridge ports leave the DTP straight into the responders, so the DTP boundary has no address decoder and no unmapped address, and an interconnect's decode-miss and default-slave behaviour is SoC-level verification
* The read status captures DECERR in op[1:0], and the read-data field returns the RDATA of the errored beat (the bridge latches the beat's data with its status): the word the responder drove on that beat, not the preloaded slot value
* Bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent legal read on every target

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. On each target's responder, preload a nonzero word in a beat-aligned slot and arm a one-shot DECERR read response there.
. Drive a JTAG2AXI single-op read of that slot on the bridge; poll the op-status until not Busy and confirm it reports DECERR.
. Confirm the read-data field equals the RDATA the passive monitor observed on the errored beat and differs from the preloaded word.
. Issue a follow-on legal read of a preloaded slot on the same bridge and confirm OKAY with matching data.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_decerr_rd_status_chk` |The read status reports DECERR for the responder-injected response on every target, and `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED` classifies the observed DECERR as armed
|`j2a_decerr_rd_rdata_chk` |`CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA`: returned read data equals the RDATA returned on the errored beat and differs from the preloaded slot value
|`j2a_decerr_rd_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the preloaded data with OKAY (`CHK-AXI-RDATA`)
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker, with `CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA` recorded by the sequence into the same finalization; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: Every bridge reports the injected DECERR, the read-data field carries the errored beat's RDATA, and a follow-on legal read succeeds on every target, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-435-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_mixed_test]]
===== 4.35 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_mixed_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Interleaved clean and DECERR-faulted accesses on every bridge

*Test Coverage*:

* The bridge AXI ports leave the DTP boundary straight into the bench responders, so there is no decoder or default subordinate at this level; each responder is configured to answer DECERR for one slot while the neighbouring slot stays clean
* Clean (OKAY) and faulted (DECERR) accesses alternate back to back on each of the three bridges, reads and writes
* Per-access status is preserved across the interleave
* No cross-contamination: a faulted access neither disturbs the clean slot's data nor corrupts the status of the adjacent clean access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build an interleaved access stream through the JTAG2AXI path:
a. Alternate accesses to a clean slot with accesses to the slot the responder is configured to fault with DECERR, covering both reads and writes.
. Drive the stream and capture per-access outcomes:
a. Confirm each clean access completes with OKAY and the expected data or side effect.
b. Confirm each faulted access reports DECERR in the SINGLE_OP status.
. Verify ordering and isolation:
a. Confirm status is preserved across the interleave and that the clean slot's data is unaffected by the interleaved faulted accesses.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_mixed_status_chk` |Each clean access reports OKAY and each faulted access reports DECERR
|`j2a_mixed_order_chk` |Per-access status preserved across the interleave
|`j2a_mixed_isolation_chk` |Clean slot data unaffected by the interleaved faulted accesses
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: The interleave exercises the clean path and the responder-injected DECERR path on every bridge with correct per-access status, and the clean slot's data is uncontaminated.

'''''

[[sec-436-dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test]]
===== 4.36 `dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Series corner cases across all bridges

*Test Coverage*:

* Sticky status accumulation and clear behavior over multi-beat series operations
* Address bookkeeping for fixed and incrementing series, including aligned and unaligned start addresses
* Per-bridge independence: each enabled bridge tracks its own series state without aliasing the others
* The bench does not check the series FIFO depth corners or `SERIES_CTRL.pl_depth` read pipelining; series operations are judged by the sticky status and the address bookkeeping

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each of the three enabled JTAG2AXI bridges, drive a sweep of series operations through `OcahJtagMasterDriver`:
a. Aligned and unaligned start addresses to exercise address bookkeeping.
b. Fixed and incrementing series to exercise both address-progression modes.
. Capture per-operation results from the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate:
a. Confirm sticky status accumulates correctly across the series and clears as defined.
b. Confirm the final address matches the expected fixed/incrementing progression for each series length.
. Verify per-bridge isolation: repeat across all three bridges and confirm no bridge's series state, status, or address bookkeeping aliases another.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_series_status_chk` |Sticky status accumulates and clears correctly across each series operation
|`j2a_series_addr_chk` |Final address matches the expected fixed/incrementing progression for aligned and unaligned starts
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features.

*Pass Criteria*: The sticky status and the fixed and incrementing address bookkeeping behave as expected on each of the three bridges, with no cross-bridge aliasing and zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-437-dtp_jtag2axi_dbg_disable_matrix_test]]
===== 4.37 `dtp_jtag2axi_dbg_disable_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Debug-disable matrix over the three JTAG2AXI bridge gate fields

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic one-hot rows for `smc_jtag2axi`, `smc_otp_jtag2axi`, and `sep_otp_jtag2axi`, plus the all-clear and all-disabled boundary masks and seeded multi-hot masks
* Per row and target: an allowed bridge completes a write+readback with real request activity; a blocked bridge shows zero request activity from a counter snapshot taken before the gated TDR write, puts no transaction inside a scoreboard blocked window held across the disable release, and keeps its RAM sentinel intact both while gated and after the release (no delayed replay), then recovers with a sanctioned operation
* Mixed rows prove unrelated-target isolation (an enabled bridge keeps working while another is gated)
* Every checker-verified outcome samples its `dbg_disable_cg` cell; the test requires all six bridge cells and emits the JSON coverage artifact

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each row mask (one-hot per field, all-clear, all-disabled, seeded multi-hot): drive the full disable vector and settle four toggling TCK cycles.
. For each bridge target under the row mask: if enabled, complete a write+readback with activity-counter evidence; if gated, preload a RAM sentinel, snapshot the request counters, open a blocked window on the target's stream, shift a full-strobe write request, and verify the counters unchanged and the sentinel intact.
. After each row with gated targets: clear all disables without reset, verify the counters are still unchanged from the pre-attempt snapshot, close each blocked window with zero transactions inside it, verify every sentinel is still intact (no delayed replay), then complete a sanctioned recovery write+readback per previously-gated target.
. Emit the `dbg_disable_cg` JSON artifact and require all six bridge cells.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`j2a_matrix_allow_chk` |An enabled bridge completes write+readback with real request activity
|`j2a_matrix_block_chk` |A gated bridge's request counters are unchanged from the snapshot taken before its TDR write, and the RAM sentinel is intact
|`j2a_matrix_noreplay_chk` |After the disables release the counters are still unchanged from that snapshot, the blocked window held across the release closes with zero monitored transactions, and the sentinels are intact (no delayed replay)
|`j2a_matrix_recovery_chk` |A sanctioned operation succeeds on every previously-gated bridge
|`j2a_matrix_isolation_chk` |Enabled bridges keep working while others are gated
|===

*Evidence*: cocotb: `CHK-AXI-BLOCKED`, `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-NOACT`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` required and finalized by the shared AXI checker; SV-UVM adds `CHK-AXI-GATE-AR`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-AW`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-W`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA` and the `jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` scoreboard features. The blocked path's positive control is the enabled baseline access of the same scenario. The checker-gated ledger requires the six bridge cells and writes the JSON artifact.

*Pass Criteria*: Every row mask produces the per-field allowed/blocked outcomes with no delayed replay and full recovery, closing all six bridge `dbg_disable_cg` cells, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-5-stap-3dcr-and-ijtag-scenarios]]
==== 5. STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG Scenarios

[[sec-51-dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test]]
===== 5.1 `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: All SIBs OFF

*Test Coverage*:

* SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) with every SIB control bit driven to 0 yields the minimum-length scan chain
* DR chain length equals the SIB-control bits only (3 bits for sec_dft, dft, dfd) with none of the bench's instrument stubs (4, 5, 6 bits behind sec_dft, dft, dfd) inserted
* None of `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o` assert during Shift-DR
* Closed SIBs bypass their instrument stubs so no stub capture/update occurs, whatever disable mask is in force (the stubs are the bench's 4-, 5-, and 6-bit registers behind the SIBs; see Verification Scope)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) into the PTAP via `OcahJtagMasterDriver` and program a seeded non-zero SIB pattern, so the all-off result is a transition from open SIBs.
. Shift a composed SIB pattern with all three SIB bits = 0 and pulse Update-DR; check the captured bits against the SIB model.
. Measure the active DR chain length.
a. Shift a seeded marker word ahead of the chain image and locate its MSB in the TDO stream.
b. Verify the latency equals the 3 SIB-control bits only (no instrument register widths added).
. Under a temporal window over the whole scan, verify no SIB select pulses (every stub stays out of the chain); the window also records the DUT's Shift-DR cycles, so a TAP that never shifted cannot satisfy the quiet checks.
. Repeat the all-off programming under a seeded per-SIB disable mask and verify the same outcome.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sib_all_off_len_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-LEN`: the marker latency through the SELECT_IJTAG chain equals the 3 SIB bits with every instrument stub bypassed
|`sib_all_off_ctrl_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: no DFD/DFT/secure-DFT `select` pulses during the observe scan, and a gated SIB's `shift_en`/`capture_en`/`update_en` stay quiet
|`sib_all_off_data_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: the captured SIB states (all 0) and the marker passthrough match the SIB reference model on every scan, including the transition from the seeded open set
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-SCAN-LEN` (the marker latency through the three closed SIBs), `CHK-SCAN-WIN` (no instrument select and no gated scan-control pulse across the observe scans, under the all-clear and the seeded disable mask), `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` (the captured SIB states and the marker passthrough against the SIB model), `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR` required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN` and `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` from the env checker, the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature, and `CHK-TAP-STATE`.

*Pass Criteria*: Chain length collapses to the SIB-control bits, all instrument scan controls stay inactive, and observed data matches the SIB reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-52-dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test]]
===== 5.2 `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: All SIBs ON

*Test Coverage*:

* SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) with all SIB bits = 1 produces the full-length iJTAG scan chain
* DR chain length equals sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument register widths (3 SIBs {plus} 3 instruments)
* All three scan controls (`jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o`) assert during Shift-DR
* Asserting all three SIB disables (`dbg_disable_i.dft_secure`, `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure`, `dbg_disable_i.dfd`) forces the SIBs closed even when the scan pattern requests all-ON, collapsing the chain
* Access is restored when the disables clear

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Shift a pattern enabling all SIBs (all bits = 1); Update-DR.
. Measure DR chain length by shifting a known marker and counting the delay.
a. Verify length equals the sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument register widths.
. Verify all three host scan-control outputs assert during Shift-DR and instrument data is captured correctly per the SIB model.
. Security gating:
a. Assert `dbg_disable_i.{dft_secure,dft_nonsecure,dfd} = 1`.
b. Re-shift the all-ON pattern; Update-DR.
c. Verify chain length collapses to SIB bits only and no instrument scan control pulses across the whole gated window.
. Clear the disables; re-shift all-ON and confirm full chain and all scan controls restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sib_all_on_len_chk` |Full chain length matches sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument widths
|`sib_all_on_ctrl_chk` |All three host scan-control outputs assert during Shift-DR
|`sib_all_on_gate_chk` |Asserted SIB disables force every SIB closed; chain collapses despite all-ON request
|`sib_all_on_restore_chk` |Full chain and scan controls restored after the disables clear
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-SCAN-LEN` (the full chain, then the collapsed chain under the three disables, then the full chain again), `CHK-SCAN-WIN` (the three instrument selects active while open, every control quiet while gated), `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` (the captured SIB and instrument states against the SIB model), `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR` required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN` and `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` from the env checker, the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature, and `CHK-TAP-STATE`.

*Pass Criteria*: The all-ON chain reaches full length with every scan control active, collapses to SIB bits when the SIB disables are asserted, and recovers when they clear, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-53-dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test]]
===== 5.3 `dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: Exhaustive/random SIB patterns

*Test Coverage*:

* All 8 SIB on/off patterns (sec_dft x dft x dfd) walked exhaustively to close `ijtag_sib_pattern` coverage
* Predicted chain length matches sum of (SIB bit {plus} instrument stub width when SIB ON and ungated, else SIB bit only); the stubs are 4, 5, 6 bits wide behind sec_dft, dft, dfd, so every open subset has a distinct length
* Only enabled, ungated instruments receive their host scan-control assertions
* Randomized per-SIB disables (`dft_secure`, `dft_nonsecure`, `dfd`) force gated SIBs closed regardless of scan pattern
* Capture/update data tracks the SIB reference model across mixed configurations: a SIB captures its effective state, an open instrument stub captures the value the previous scan latched into it

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagMasterDriver`.
. Walk all 8 SIB patterns exhaustively:
a. Shift each (sec_dft, dft, dfd) pattern composed over the current chain, with seeded values for the instruments already open; Update-DR.
b. Shift a seeded marker word ahead of the chain image, locate its MSB in the TDO stream, and verify the latency equals SIB bits plus the widths of enabled instruments.
c. Under a temporal window over that scan, verify `select` pulses only for the enabled instruments.
. For 16 additional randomized iterations:
a. Drive a random per-SIB disable mask and a random SIB pattern; Update-DR.
b. Verify gated SIBs (those with their `dbg_disable_i` field asserted) stay closed despite the requested pattern.
c. Verify chain length accounts for forced-closed SIBs and only non-gated enabled instruments receive scan control.
. Compare the captured bits of every scan against the OCAH-local SIB model for each configuration.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sib_random_len_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-LEN`: the marker latency through the SELECT_IJTAG chain matches the predicted SIB-bit {plus} ungated-instrument-width sum
|`sib_random_ctrl_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`: `select` pulses only for enabled, ungated instruments; a closed SIB's `select` never pulses
|`sib_random_gate_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN` and `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: a gated SIB's `select`/`shift_en`/`capture_en`/`update_en` never pulse and it captures closed under random disable masks
|`sib_random_data_chk` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`: the captured SIB states and instrument registers, and the marker passthrough, match the SIB reference model on every scan
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-SCAN-LEN` (the marker latency for each of the eight patterns and each seeded pattern under its disable mask), `CHK-SCAN-WIN` (selects only for open, ungated instruments; a gated SIB's four controls quiet), `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` (every capture against the SIB model, including the value an open instrument latched on the previous scan), `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR` required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN` and `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` from the env checker, the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature, and `CHK-TAP-STATE`.

*Pass Criteria*: All 8 SIB patterns plus randomized security combinations yield correct chain lengths, scan-control assertions, and gating behavior matching the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-54-dtp_ijtag_dft_test]]
===== 5.4 `dtp_ijtag_dft_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: DFT secure/non-secure access

*Test Coverage*:

* Independent access to the secure and non-secure DFT instruments via their SIBs (SELECT_IJTAG, IR=0x1A)
* Both DFT SIBs may be open simultaneously (no hardware mutual exclusion); chain length sums both instruments
* `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure = 1` gates the non-secure DFT SIB; `dbg_disable_i.dft_secure = 1` gates the secure DFT SIB
* Gating one DFT SIB leaves the other accessible (cross-resource isolation), a gated Update-DR does not modify stored SIB state, and access resumes without TAP reset once the disable clears

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A); enable the non-secure DFT SIB; Update-DR.
a. Verify `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts and shift a test pattern through the non-secure chain.
. Disable non-secure, enable secure DFT SIB; Update-DR.
a. Verify `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts and shift a test pattern through the secure chain.
. Enable both DFT SIBs; Update-DR; verify both scan controls assert and chain length equals the sum of both instruments.
. Secure gating: assert `dbg_disable_i.dft_secure=1`; verify the secure DFT SIB forced closed (its scan controls never pulse across the gated window) while the non-secure SIB remains openable; clear.
. Non-secure gating: assert `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure=1`; verify the non-secure DFT SIB forced closed while the secure SIB remains openable; clear.
. Stored-state evidence: attempt to open a gated SIB, release the disable without reset, verify the gated open attempt did not stick, then confirm a sanctioned open succeeds.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`dft_access_chk` |Secure and non-secure DFT chains each accessible via their SIB with correct scan controls
|`dft_parallel_chk` |Both DFT SIBs open simultaneously; chain length sums both instruments
|`dft_nonsecure_gate_chk` |Non-secure DFT SIB forced closed under `dbg_disable_i.dft_nonsecure`
|`dft_secure_gate_chk` |Secure DFT SIB forced closed under `dbg_disable_i.dft_secure`
|`dft_isolation_chk` |Gating one DFT SIB leaves the other accessible
|`dft_gate_restore_chk` |Both DFT SIBs restored, without TAP reset, after their disables clear
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-SCAN-LEN` (each DFT instrument alone and both together), `CHK-SCAN-WIN` (the secure and non-secure selects while open; a gated SIB's controls quiet while the other stays openable), `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` (the captures against the SIB model, the gated open attempt not stuck after release), `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR` required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN` and `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` from the env checker, the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature, and `CHK-TAP-STATE`.

*Pass Criteria*: Secure and non-secure DFT instruments are independently reachable in the same scan flow, each gated by exactly its own disable field and restored when it clears, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-55-dtp_ijtag_dfd_test]]
===== 5.5 `dtp_ijtag_dfd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: DFD access

*Test Coverage*:

* DFD instrument reachable through the iJTAG network when its SIB is open (SELECT_IJTAG, IR=0x1A)
* `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts shift control and data shifts correctly through `jtag_dfd_host_scan_out_o` / `jtag_dfd_host_scan_in_i`
* `dbg_disable_i.dfd = 1` forces the DFD SIB closed (gated), restored when the disable clears

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagMasterDriver`; open only the DFD SIB; Update-DR.
. Verify `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts; shift a known pattern out on `jtag_dfd_host_scan_out_o` and read it back through `jtag_dfd_host_scan_in_i` via the OCAH-local SIB model.
. Assert `dbg_disable_i.dfd = 1`; attempt to open the DFD SIB.
a. Verify the SIB stays closed and none of the DFD scan controls pulse across the whole gated window.
. Clear the disable without reset; verify the gated open attempt did not stick, then re-open the DFD SIB and verify DFD access is restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`dfd_access_chk` |DFD scan data shifts correctly when the SIB is open and ungated
|`dfd_gate_chk` |DFD SIB forced closed and its scan controls never pulse while `dbg_disable_i.dfd` is asserted
|`dfd_stored_chk` |A gated Update-DR does not modify stored SIB state (no delayed open after release)
|`dfd_restore_chk` |DFD access resumes, without TAP reset, after the disable clears
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-SCAN-LEN` (the DFD instrument open), `CHK-SCAN-WIN` (the DFD select while open, every DFD control quiet while gated, the select again after release), `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` (the DFD captures against the SIB model, the gated open attempt not stuck), `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR` required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN` and `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` from the env checker, the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature, and `CHK-TAP-STATE`.

*Pass Criteria*: DFD is reachable when ungated and forced closed when `dbg_disable_i.dfd` is asserted, recovering without reset when the disable clears, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-56-dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test]]
===== 5.6 `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (primary TAP master and the downstream slave TAPs behind every STAP host port), OCAH-local STAP chain model

*Objective*: Downstream (I/O) STAP selection proved end to end against a downstream TAP

*Test Coverage*:

* TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) selects the I/O STAP by writing 3DCR `stap_sel=1`, `tms_hold=1`, `config_hold=1` through composed full-chain scans (the `TAP_3DCR` data register is the 2-bit PTAP 3DCR followed serially by the STAP SIB chain); a selected I/O STAP additionally splices one extra flop into the chain through its TDI lockup latch; every scan issued while a STAP is selected is composed over the full network, IR scans included, because the PTAP routes its instruction shift-out into the STAP chain
* A behavioral downstream TAP (`ocah_jtag_vip` slave device: 5-bit IR, distinct IDCODE, one writable `DS_TDR` of a distinct width) is spliced behind every STAP host port; its IR and selected data register become chain segments the reference model predicts independently of the device engine
* While selected, the downstream IDCODE reads back through the STAP, a network-wide IR scan selects `DS_TDR`, and a per-pass random value written through the chain reads back and is confirmed by the device's Update-DR record; the I/O host port forwards (`tdo_oen` pulses, `tms` follows the live TMS) as corroboration
* `dbg_disable_i.stap_io = 1` stops forwarding, parks the host `tms` at the stored `tms_hold=1` so the downstream TAP sits in Test-Logic-Reset, blocks a 3DCR update attempted while gated, and leaves the downstream register unchanged with no Update-DR recorded
* Once the disable clears, without any reset, the stored selection resumes: the downstream answers IDCODE again (park reset proven) and reloading `DS_TDR` returns the pre-gate value; an unrelated STAP's downstream TAP completes a full load/write/read cycle while the target stays gated and records no update; a fresh configuration recovers fully

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Flush the TAP_3DCR configuration chain with one over-length all-zero scan, then configure the I/O STAP with composed scans: open its SIB, then write its 3DCR `stap_sel=1, tms_hold=1, config_hold=1` (PTAP 3DCR held at `select=1, config_hold=1`).
. Run a state-maintaining chain scan inside a temporal window; verify the host `tdo_oen` pulses, host `tms` follows the live TMS, and the captured chain matches the reference model.
. Read the downstream IDCODE through the chain (`CHK-DS-IDCODE`); select `DS_TDR` with a composed IR scan; write a per-pass random value; verify the device recorded exactly one Update-DR with that value (`CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`); read it back through the chain (`CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`).
. Assert `dbg_disable_i.stap_io = 1` and attempt a randomized deselecting 3DCR write:
a. Verify `tdo_oen` never pulses and `tms` parks at the stored `tms_hold=1` across the whole gated window.
b. Verify the chain readback shows the update was ignored (the STAP captures its masked `stap_sel` as 0 while the stored payload survives).
c. Verify the downstream device recorded no Update-DR, still holds the written value (`CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`), and sits in Test-Logic-Reset (`CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`).
. Clear the disable without reset; verify forwarding resumes from the stored selection, the downstream answers IDCODE again, and reloading `DS_TDR` reads back the pre-gate value (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`).
. Re-assert the disable and configure a neighbor STAP; verify the neighbor forwards while the gated port stays quiet, the neighbor's downstream TAP completes a load/write/read cycle, and the gated port's device records no update (isolation).
. Clear all disables, flush (composed IR load parking the spliced downstream TAPs on BYPASS), and configure fresh; verify full recovery with a new `DS_TDR` write and readback.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`stap_ds_select_chk` |Selected I/O STAP forwards: `tdo_oen` pulses and `tms` follows the live TMS (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`)
|`stap_ds_readback_chk` |Composed chain readback matches the reference model in every state (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
|`stap_ds_downstream_chk` |Downstream IDCODE and `DS_TDR` read back through the selected STAP; the device's Update-DR record matches the write (`CHK-DS-IDCODE`, `CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`)
|`stap_ds_gate_chk` |With `dbg_disable_i.stap_io` asserted, forwarding stops, a gated 3DCR update is ignored, and the downstream register is frozen in Test-Logic-Reset (`CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`, `CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`)
|`stap_ds_isolation_chk` |An unrelated STAP and its downstream TAP stay usable while the I/O STAP is gated
|`stap_ds_recover_chk` |Selection resumes without reset after release with the pre-gate downstream value intact (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`), and a fresh configuration fully recovers
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-SCAN-WIN` (the I/O host port forwarding while selected, quiet and parked while gated, the neighbor forwarding while the I/O port stays gated), `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` (every composed chain readback against the STAP model), `CHK-DS-IDCODE`, `CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`, `CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`, `CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`, `CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME` (the downstream TAP behind the I/O port and behind the neighbor), `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR` required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN` and `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` from the env checker, the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature, and `CHK-TAP-STATE`.

*Pass Criteria*: The I/O STAP forwards only while selected and enabled, the downstream TAP behind it is programmable and readable exactly then, `dbg_disable_i.stap_io` alone gates it with downstream state preserved and no delayed effect, and recovery needs no reset, with every required `CHK-*` ID recorded and zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`stap_smc_select_chk` |Selected SMC debug STAP forwards: `tdo_oen` pulses and `tms` follows the live TMS (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`)
|`stap_smc_readback_chk` |Composed chain readback matches the reference model in every state (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`)
