b. Write the active `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` registers via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent` and read each back.
c. Drive CTP triggers via the OCAH-local CTP BFM with randomized timing.
d. Observe CLA destinations via the OCAH-local CTM monitor and verify the selected CLA channels respond.
e. Sample the source x destination coverage of the exercised CTP-to-CLA combinations.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_csr_chk` |Every randomized CTP-to-CLA mask reads back as programmed
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_route_chk` |Selected CLA destinations respond per the reference model across iterations
|`ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_nospur_chk` |No non-selected CLA destination toggles
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-XTRIG-CSR`, `CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION` (per iteration: every selected output's select readback, the routed pulses, the fired set against the CTM model, no unselected output and every selected output idle at the window's end) required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds the `xtrig_csr` scoreboard feature (`CHK-SB-XTRIG-CSR`).

*Pass Criteria*: Every random CTP-to-CLA configuration routes correctly to its selected CLA destinations with no spurious activity and full route-combination coverage, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-627-dtp_ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test]]
===== 6.27 `dtp_ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM BFM

*Objective*: CTM CSR deterministic sweep

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic closure of CTM generated-regblock byte-enable and masked-write condition holes that random selection can miss
* Full-word, per-byte, and masked writes to `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` with readback after each
* Reserved bits read back as zero and are unaffected by writes
* Internal route decode tracks each programmed `CT_DST_SELECT` value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle and disable all CTM source routing.
. Sweep every `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` deterministically via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Apply the full-word patterns (single bits, the full select mask, a fixed and a seeded multi-bit mask, the all-ones word, and the inverted patterns, which drive reserved bits [31:26] to 1), then every byte strobe (0x1, 0x2, 0x4, 0x8) with a seeded word over a seeded base.
b. Read back after each write and compare the full word: the defined field holds the expected value and the reserved bits read 0.
. For two seeded output registers holding the seeded multi-bit select, pulse an input the select names and see the output fire per the reference model, then pulse an input it does not name and see the output stay quiet.
. Disable all CTM source routing before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_csr_chk` |Full-word and byte-strobe readbacks, compared as the full word, match the expected `CT_DST_SELECT` values
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_rsvd_chk` |Reserved bits [31:26] read 0 after the all-ones, inverted, and strobed writes
|`ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_route_chk` |Two swept output registers holding a multi-bit select fire for an input the select names and stay quiet for one it does not, against the reference model
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-XTRIG-CSR` (every full-word, inverted, and byte-strobe readback of each CT_SRC select with the reserved bits zero), `CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION` (the two swept outputs firing for an input the select names and quiet for one it does not) required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds the `xtrig_csr` scoreboard feature (`CHK-SB-XTRIG-CSR`).

*Pass Criteria*: Every byte-enable and masked-write condition in the CTM regblock is exercised and reads back correctly with reserved bits clear and route decode matching, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-628-dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test]]
===== 6.28 `dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM BFM

*Objective*: CTM all-source select coverage

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic closure of per-source select mapping for every defined CTM source-select CSR (`CT_SRC[0].CONFIG_0` through `CT_SRC[25].CONFIG_0`)
* Single-destination, paired-destination, and inverted-destination `CT_DST_SELECT` masks written and read back at every source index
* No source index aliases another CTM source register
* Direct exercise of all generated CTM source-select mapping code in the default DTP configuration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert power-on reset, bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle, and disable all CTM source routing.
. For every defined CTM source index, via `OcahAxiLiteMasterAgent`:
a. Write a single-destination `CT_DST_SELECT` mask, read back, and compare the defined destination-select field.
b. Write a paired-destination mask, read back, and compare.
c. Write an inverted-destination mask, read back, and compare.
d. Confirm the written value affects only this source register and no neighboring `+CT_SRC[N]_CONFIG_0+` (no aliasing).
. Sample per-source select coverage so every `CT_SRC1..25_CONFIG_0` mapping is touched.
. Disable all CTM source routing again before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_csr_chk` |Every defined source-select CSR accepts and returns the single/paired/inverted mask
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_alias_chk` |No source index aliases another CTM source register
|`ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_route_chk` |Per-source route decode matches the programmed mask against the reference model
|===

*Evidence*: `CHK-XTRIG-CSR` (the single, paired, and inverted masks read back at every source-select register and the neighbors unchanged) required in both realizations; SV-UVM adds the `xtrig_csr` scoreboard feature (`CHK-SB-XTRIG-CSR`).

*Pass Criteria*: Every defined CTM source-select CSR is touched with single, paired, and inverted masks and reads back correctly with no aliasing across source indices, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[dtp-formal-plan]]
=== Formal Verification Plan

Formal property checking corroborates the control-logic rows of the
traceability matrix; it does not replace the simulation credit. The open-path
flow runs SymbiYosys through the runner's formal mode
(`tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification) and follows the property
style in `hw/common/dv/docs/formal-property-style.adoc`. `dtp_formal_cfg.toml`
and `testlists/formal.toml` are the launch entry; the properties, the bind
files, the task files and the reset environments live under `formal/`. The
plan names nine targets, T1 to T9, over three formal tops: `dtp` for the
JTAG-domain blocks, `jtag2axi` for the bridge control, and
`cross_trigger_network` for the single-clock cross-trigger logic. Every
target row names the traceability rows it corroborates, the app checks that
apply, the path that runs them, and the item `run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode
formal` launches. The `dtp` blocks share `formal/fpv/sby/dtp.sby`, whose task
tags select one block's property module; `dtp_jtag2axi.sby` and
`dtp_cross_trigger_network.sby` beside it carry the other two tops, and each top
has its own environment module.

[[dtp-formal-targets]]
==== Targets

A target is a described block whose behaviour is a boolean over a small
control state: a state machine, a handshake, a gating term, a register with a
fixed access policy. Data movement is out (<<dtp-formal-exclusions>>).

[cols="1,3,3,2,2",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Described block |Rows |Formal top and item |Path
|T1 TAP controller |`jtag_tap_ctrlr`: the sixteen-state Moore machine, the saturating TMS counter, the scan-control flags |F1.1, F1.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_tap_fpv` |Open
|T2 Instruction register and TDR selection |`jtag_inst_reg` (6-bit shift, 64-bit one-hot decode, IDCODE on reset) and the PTAP select and TDO multiplexer terms: bypass for undefined opcodes and for disabled features, one TDR selected per instruction, zero-length bypass, the read-only IDCODE and CAPS captures |F1.3, F1.4, F1.5 (selection), F2.5 |`dtp`, `dtp_ir_fpv` |Open
|T3 Reset hierarchy and persistence |`jtag_trst_n = trst_n AND pwr_on_rst_ni`, `rst_n = !test_logic_reset`, `chrst_n` under `jtag_tmp`; `jtag_tmp_status_reg`; `jtag_ic_reset_reg` with `reset_hold`; `jtag_3dcr_reg` with `config_hold` |F1.2, F1.6, F2.1, F2.2 |`dtp`, `dtp_reset_fpv` |Open
|T4 STAP selection and debug disable |The PTAP 3DCR select, the four `jtag_stap` instances (I/O, SMC, SEP, extra) with their 3DCR and lockup flops, the `stap_host` return path in `jtag_intf_unit`, the two-flop `dbg_disable_i` synchronizers |F3.3, F3.4, F3.5, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_stap_fpv` |Open; STAP chain order and disable wiring also as licensed connectivity
|T5 iJTAG SIB network and debug disable |The three `prim_jtag_sib_mux_post` instances (DFT secure, DFT non-secure, DFD) |F3.1, F3.2, F3.6 |`dtp`, `dtp_sib_fpv` |Open
|T6 Debug control and clock stop |`jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` with its `cla_clock_stop` synchronizer; `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |F2.3, F2.4, F5.7 |`dtp`, `dtp_dbgctl_fpv`; `cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_clkstop_fpv` |Open
|T7 JTAG2AXI bridge control |`jtag2axi`: the seven-state request machine, the TCK-side admit counters, the ACLK outstanding counters, the status encoder, the disable gate and its flush |F4.3 (status encoding), F4.4 (control), F4.5 |`jtag2axi`, `jtag2axi_ctrl_fpv` |Open for the control with the AXI side free; the CDC transport and the mid-transaction reset as licensed X-propagation and reset checks
|T8 Cross-trigger port and matrix |`ctp_handshake_ctrl` (sender and receiver), `ctp_core` mode multiplexers and wire-OR edge detect, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, one `ctm_src_selector` slice |F5.1, F5.2, F5.3, F5.6 |`cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_xtrig_fpv` |Open
|T9 Cross-trigger CSR access |The CTN address map into the AXI-Lite crossbar and its error subordinate; the generated register blocks' field access, strobes and reset values |F5.4, F5.5 |`cross_trigger_network`, `ctn_csr_fpv` |Open for the address-map contract and the field policy of one CTP and one CTM slice; the full register sweep derived from the SystemRDL as the licensed register app
|===

[[dtp-formal-exclusions]]
==== Exclusions

[cols="3,4",options="header",]
|===
|Logic |Reason and where the credit lives
|JTAG2AXI data path: the 132-bit shared shift register with a variable active length, the byte-lane shifts by `addr mod BEAT_BYTES`, the 56-bit series address increment and its wrap, the `DEADBEEF` pending-read capture |Wide arithmetic and per-bit loops; a bounded proof over them is a data check the F4.1 and F4.2 scenarios make with data verification. The bridge target proves control only.
|The five gray-coded clearable CDC FIFOs with three-stage synchronizers and the coupled reset controller inside `axi_cdc_clearable` |Third-party IP with its own correctness argument; the open path takes the AXI side as free inputs. A reset that lands mid-transaction leaves the request machine waiting with no recovery other than Test-Logic-Reset; that path is a licensed reset and X-propagation check, not an open-path property.
|Boundary-scan data and the external iJTAG instrument chains |Loopbacks in `tb_top`; the design carries selection and gating only, which T2 and T5 cover.
|`ctp_pulse_stretcher` at large `STRETCH_MULT` |A 16-bit down counter, up to 65 536 cycles. End-to-end proofs assume `STRETCH_MULT <= 4`; the counter's reload and terminal behaviour is one inductive property of its own.
|The full 26 x 26 cross-trigger matrix |A generate of identical selector slices; one slice with a free select mask stands for the array.
|The AXI-Lite crossbar internals (demux address-write lock, per-port FIFOs) |Vendor IP; T9 checks the address-map contract at the CTN port and the register blocks behind it.
|The pad-ring wire-OR bus |The shared wire, pull-up and inversion contract live outside the RTL; a wire-OR property needs an environment model of the bus and is not a DUT property.
|TDO retiming and the lockup flops |Negedge flops are ordinary state under `multiclock on`; the timing margin they buy is not a property.
|Deriving `dbg_disable_t`, the SMU-composed IC_RESET external slice, real downstream TAPs |Integration level, as for the simulation bench.
|===

[[dtp-formal-properties]]
==== Properties by target

Every label follows the style chapter: `ast_` assertions, `asm_` assumptions,
`cov_` covers, each a boolean over current and `$past` values. Priority P0
marks the properties that need an unbounded proof (`prove`) before any
release; P1 and P2 sign off on a bounded result at the target's depth
(<<dtp-formal-depths>>). The P0 set is the security-gating set plus the reset
hierarchy: every disable field gates exactly its path, and no reset source
reaches a register the standard reserves for another.

T1 is the property module in `formal/props/dtp_tap_props.sv`, bound to
`jtag_tap_ctrlr` by `formal/props/dtp_tap_bind.sv`:

[cols="3,3,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Label |Checks |Row |Prio
|`ast_tap_state_one_hot` |The state register holds exactly one state |F1.1 |P0
|`ast_tap_five_tms_high_reach_tlr` |A saturated TMS counter implies Test-Logic-Reset |F1.2 |P0
|`ast_tap_tlr_holds_on_tms_high`, `ast_tap_tlr_exits_to_rti`, `ast_tap_rti_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_shift_holds_on_tms_low`, `ast_tap_update_dr_only_from_exit`, `ast_tap_update_ir_only_from_exit` |The legal TMS arcs into and out of the reset, idle, shift and update states |F1.1 |P1
|`ast_tap_select_exclusive`, `ast_tap_moore_flags_follow_state`, `ast_tap_negedge_flags_follow_state` |The DR and IR selects are exclusive; the Moore flags and the negedge-clocked scan-control flags follow the state |F1.1 |P1
|===

The T1 item also binds the shared JTAG checker to `dtp` on the primary TAP
(`formal/props/dtp_jtag_fv_bind.sv`, `hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_jtag_vip/sva/ocah_jtag_fv.sv`),
whose slave rules it asserts: `ast_jtag_state_onehot`,
`ast_jtag_state_next_legal` (every transition follows the IEEE 1149.1
controller diagram), `ast_jtag_trst_tlr`, `ast_jtag_tlr_tms5`,
`ast_jtag_tdo_oen_shift_only` and `ast_jtag_tdo_stable_while_tck_high` (the
enable, and TDO while the enable is active, change on the falling edge alone),
with the covers `cov_jtag_shift_dr`, `cov_jtag_shift_ir`,
`cov_jtag_tlr_from_five_tms_high` and `cov_jtag_tdo_driven`. The T7 and T9
items bind the shared AXI checker
(`hw/common/dv/vip/ocah_axi_vip/sva/ocah_axi_fv.sv`) with the design's side
asserted: on `jtag2axi` the request machine's master rules,
`ast_axi_<ch>_valid_reset_low`, `ast_axi_<ch>_valid_held`,
`ast_axi_<ch>_payload_stable` and `ast_axi_<ch>_within_capacity` for the AW,
W and AR channels plus the AW and AR burst-legality rules; on
`cross_trigger_network` the slave rules `ast_axi_<ch>_valid_reset_low`,
`ast_axi_<ch>_valid_held` and `ast_axi_<ch>_payload_stable` for the B and R
channels, `ast_axi_b_not_before_aw`, `ast_axi_r_not_before_ar`,
`ast_axi_axil_b_resp_legal` and `ast_axi_axil_r_resp_legal`, with the five
`cov_axi_<ch>_handshake` covers (<<dtp-formal-assumptions>>). Every report
names them under the bind instance, for example
`u_ocah_axi_fv.gen_axi_b_valid_held.ast_axi_b_valid_held`.

The remaining targets, one property module per block under
`formal/props/`, with the intents the labels carry. T2 and T3 bind to
`jtag_ptap`, T4 and T5 to `jtag_intf_unit` with the four STAPs and the three
SIBs packed into per-instance vectors, T6 to `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` and
`ctn_clock_stop_ctrl`, T7 to `jtag2axi`, and T8 and T9 to
`cross_trigger_network` with external port 0 and matrix slice 0 standing for
their arrays:

[cols="1,3,4,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Module |Property intents |Prio
|T2 |`dtp_ir_props.sv` on `jtag_inst_reg` and the PTAP select terms |`ast_ir_decoded_one_hot`: the decoded instruction is one-hot. `ast_ir_capture_loads_01`: Capture-IR loads `6'b000001`. `ast_ir_update_decodes_shift_reg`: after Update-IR the decoded instruction is `1 << shift_reg`. `ast_ir_reset_is_idcode`: Test-Logic-Reset, TRST and power-on reset leave IDCODE decoded. `ast_tdr_select_one_hot`: at most one register select is high in a DR scan, each select equals the DR select under its own instruction, the bypass register is also clocked by IDCODE, INV_BYPASS and SELECT_IJTAG scans, and the TDO multiplexer reads the register of the instruction. `ast_undefined_selects_bypass`: every opcode outside the defined set, and every defined opcode whose feature parameter is 0, selects the bypass register. `ast_zlb_bypasses_retimer`: under ZERO_LENGTH_BYPASS in Shift-DR, TDO follows TDI in the same step. `ast_idcode_caps_read_only`: the IDCODE and CAPS captures load the parameter constants and no Update-DR changes them. Covers: each of the 64 opcodes decoded, the bypass selection for one undefined opcode, a zero-length shift, a read of each capability TDR. |P1; the decode one-hot and the undefined-to-bypass rule P0
|T3 |`dtp_reset_props.sv` on the PTAP reset terms, `jtag_tmp`, `jtag_tmp_status_reg`, `jtag_ic_reset_reg`, `jtag_3dcr_reg` |`ast_trst_or_por_resets_tap`: either source low forces Test-Logic-Reset and the IDCODE instruction. `ast_tlr_asserts_rst_n`: `rst_n` is low exactly while the negedge-sampled state is Test-Logic-Reset. `ast_chrst_follows_rst_unless_persistent`: `chrst_n` equals `rst_n` while persistence is off and stays high in Test-Logic-Reset while it is on. `ast_tmp_on_only_by_clamp_hold`, `ast_tmp_off_by_release_or_escape`: the two TMP transitions and no other; the bypass escape needs Update-IR, an explicit BYPASS opcode and the escape bit. `ast_tmp_survives_tlr`: Test-Logic-Reset leaves the TMP state unchanged. `ast_ic_reset_hold_blocks_tlr`: with `reset_hold` 0, Test-Logic-Reset leaves `reset_enable` and `reset_control` unchanged; with `reset_hold` 1 it restores them to all-ones. `ast_ic_reset_trst_clears_all`: TRST restores every IC_RESET bit including `reset_hold`. `ast_ic_reset_ovrd_is_not_enable`: `.ovrd` is the inversion of `reset_enable` and `.val` equals `reset_control`. `ast_3dcr_config_hold_blocks_tlr`: the PTAP 3DCR survives Test-Logic-Reset while `config_hold` is set and clears on TRST. Covers: persistence on then off by each exit, an IC_RESET override held across a Test-Logic-Reset, a 3DCR held across a Test-Logic-Reset. |P0
|T4 |`dtp_stap_props.sv` on the PTAP 3DCR, each `jtag_stap`, and the `stap_host` terms of `jtag_intf_unit` |`ast_stap_disable_forces_sel_low`, `ast_stap_disable_tdo_low`, `ast_stap_disable_blocks_3dcr_update`: with the STAP's disable field high, `stap_sel` is 0, `host_tdo_o` is 0, and the 3DCR update register holds its value. `ast_stap_unselected_parks_tms`: with `stap_sel` 0, `host_tap_ctrl_o.tms` equals `tms_hold`; with 1 it follows the client TMS. `ast_stap_tdo_oen_only_when_selected_shifting`: `host_tdo_oen_o` is `stap_sel AND shift_en`. `ast_stap_host_disable_loops_back`: with `stap_host` disabled the PTAP scan input is the chain's own output and every host scan-control enable is 0. `ast_ptap_stap_select_routes_tdo`: with the PTAP 3DCR select set, TDO comes from the STAP chain, else from the TDR multiplexer. `ast_dbg_disable_sync_fail_closed`: each synchronizer resets to 1 and follows its input after two `tck` edges. Covers: the I/O, SMC, SEP and extra STAP each selected; a STAP disabled while selected; the return path disabled during a shift, and enabled during a shift with the PTAP select set. |P0
|T5 |`dtp_sib_props.sv` on the three SIBs |`ast_sib_disable_forces_en_low`, `ast_sib_disable_blocks_update`, `ast_sib_disable_host_quiet`: with the SIB's field high, the segment enable is 0, the SIB bit does not update, and the host `capture_en`, `shift_en`, `update_en`, `select` and `host_scan_out_o` are 0. `ast_sib_open_routes_host`: with the enable set, the client scan output is the host scan input, else the SIB's own bit. `ast_sib_host_ctrl_follows_client`: the host enables equal the client enables AND `select` while enabled. `ast_sib_chain_order`: the DFD SIB's input is the non-secure SIB's output, whose input is the secure SIB's output. Covers: every SIB pattern (eight), each SIB disabled while open. |P0
|T6 |`dtp_dbgctl_props.sv` on `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg`; `dtp_ctn_clkstop_props.sv` on `ctn_clock_stop_ctrl` |`ast_dbgctl_rw_bits_hold`: bits 3:0 change only on an Update-DR of the DEBUG_CONTROL TDR. `ast_dbgctl_ro_bit_captures_status`: bit 4 captures the synchronized `cla_clock_stop` and ignores the shifted value. `ast_dbgctl_outputs_follow_update_reg`: the four outputs equal the update register bits. `ast_clkstop_or_after_three`: `stop_clks_o` equals the OR of `jtag_clock_stop_i` and every `clk_stop_req_i` bit sampled three `clk_i` cycles earlier. `ast_cla_status_excludes_jtag`: `cla_clock_stop_o` is the OR of the request bits alone, with no register. `ast_clkstop_low_in_reset`: `stop_clks_o` is 0 while `rst_ni` is low. Covers: a JTAG stop with no CLA request, a CLA request with no JTAG stop, the boot-stall override with each stall value. |P1
|T7 |`dtp_jtag2axi_ctrl_props.sv` on `jtag2axi`, one bind serving the three instances |`ast_j2a_state_valid`: the request machine holds one of its seven states. `ast_j2a_valid_only_in_send_states`: the TCK-side `aw_valid`, `w_valid` and `ar_valid` requests are high only in the address and data states. `ast_j2a_idle_after_status`: UPDATE_STATUS returns to IDLE in one cycle. `ast_j2a_resp_only_while_waiting`: a response beat from a responder that follows the shared checker's rules arrives no earlier than the request's last handshake and no later than the state that consumes it, so it depends on the assumed responder rules and fails without them. `ast_j2a_status_encodes_resp`: OKAY, SLVERR and DECERR responses map to SUCCESS, SLVERR and DECERR, any other code to SLVERR. `ast_j2a_series_status_sticky`: a series error status holds until the series-control reset bit. `ast_j2a_admit_bounded_by_depth`: the in-flight and pushed counters never exceed the pipeline depth, itself clamped to `FIFO_DEPTH`. `ast_j2a_outstanding_saturates`: at their saturation value the ACLK outstanding counters hold the CDC ready low, so they never wrap. `ast_j2a_disable_holds_idle`: after a cycle with the disable high and the machine idle, the machine is idle, the single-request buffer, both series FIFOs and the read counters are empty, and no Update-DR latches while the disable is high. `ast_j2a_disable_inflight_completes`: a disable that arrives outside IDLE does not abort the transaction in flight: the send states keep their valids, a response completes into UPDATE_STATUS, and IDLE is entered from UPDATE_STATUS alone. `ast_j2a_release_replays_nothing`: an idle machine with an empty single buffer and request FIFO stays idle, so a released disable issues nothing until a new Update-DR. Covers: a single write and a single read to completion, a series of `FIFO_DEPTH` reads admitted, a SLVERR and a DECERR status, a disable landing in each non-idle state. |P0 for the disable and admit properties, P1 otherwise
|T8 |`dtp_ctn_xtrig_props.sv` on `ctp_handshake_ctrl`, `ctp_core`, `ctp_pulse_stretcher`, `ctm_src_selector` |`ast_hs_sender_states`, `ast_hs_receiver_states`: the two machines hold legal states and take only the four-phase arcs. `ast_hs_req_stable_until_ack`: `ct_req_out` holds until the synchronized ack. `ast_hs_ack_mirrors_req`: the receiver asserts ack on a synchronized request and drops it when the request drops. `ast_hs_dst_is_one_pulse`: `ct_dst` rises only from the idle receiver on a synchronized request and lasts one cycle, two when the request drops in the cycle after the acknowledge rose. `ast_hs_busy_lags_state`: `busy` is the registered not-idle of either machine. `ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`: `reset_i` returns the sender to IDLE with `ct_req_out` low, and leaves the receiver untouched. `ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`: the wire-OR and point-to-point pad enables are mutually exclusive and match `CONFIG.MODE`. `ast_ctp_wire_or_edge_pulse`: a rising synchronized request yields one stretcher trigger. `ast_stretch_width`: a trigger holds the output for `STRETCH_MULT + 1` cycles and a new trigger reloads the counter. `ast_ctm_slice_route`: `ct_src_o` equals the registered OR of `ct_dst AND select`, and an all-zero select isolates the output. `ast_ctp_status_reflects_pins`: `STATUS.REQ_IN` and `ACK_IN` equal the synchronized de-inverted pad values, `REQ_OUT` and `ACK_OUT` the de-inverted registered outputs; in wire-OR mode `REQ_OUT` reads 0 and `ACK_OUT` reads `INVERT`. Covers: a full four-phase cycle against the peer model, a stretched wire-OR pulse of the maximum assumed width, a route from an internal source to an external port and back, reset in each mode. |P1
|T9 |`dtp_ctn_csr_props.sv` on `cross_trigger_network` and one CTP and one CTM register block |`ast_csr_unmapped_decerr`: an address outside `0x000` to `0x2FF`, and the last byte address of every window, completes with DECERR on both channels; a mapped one completes with OKAY. `ast_csr_ctp_window`: an address in `0x200 + 16n` to `0x20F + 16n` reaches CTP `n` alone. `ast_csr_ctm_aliases`: the matrix block dispatches the port address's low byte, its readback follows address bits 7:2 alone, and the response carries the dispatched readback, so `0x100` to `0x1FF` reads the same registers as `0x000` to `0x0FF`. `ast_csr_reset_values`: CONFIG, STRETCH_MULT and every CT_DST_SELECT read 0 after reset. `ast_csr_status_write_ignored`: a write to STATUS returns OKAY and changes nothing. `ast_csr_strobe_masks_bytes`: a write changes only the strobed bytes. `ast_csr_read_after_write`: a read after a completed write returns the written field value. Covers: a DECERR write and read, a CTP and a CTM read-after-write, a masked write. |P1
|===

[[dtp-formal-observations]]
==== Observations the properties settle

Seven properties above pin RTL behaviour beyond what the design chapters
state; the seventh is a deviation from the public protocol specification
that the shared AXI checker exposes and the `jtag2axi` environment
excludes.

* A disabled bridge gates only the IDLE arm of its AXI machine and flushes
  the buffers once IDLE is reached, so a transaction already in flight
  completes (`ast_j2a_disable_inflight_completes`).
* `stop_clks_o` is the OR of the requests through a two-flop synchronizer
  and an output flop, three `clk_i` cycles (`ast_clkstop_or_after_three`).
* `INT_CT_MODE[i] = 0` is the wire-OR pulse mode,
  `MODE_WIRE_OR = ~INT_CT_MODE[i]` (`ast_ctp_mode_enables_exclusive`, checked
  per internal port).
* `STATUS.REQ_OUT` reads 0 in wire-OR mode because the pad data is static
  and the pull is on the enable; `STATUS.ACK_OUT` reads `INVERT` there, the
  de-inverted value of an output the mode never drives
  (`ast_ctp_status_reflects_pins`).
* `CONFIG.RESET` returns only the sender machine to IDLE; the receiver has no
  reset arc (`ast_hs_reset_recovers_sender`).
* The CTM is mapped over 512 bytes but decodes address bits 7:2 only, so the
  upper half of its window aliases the lower (`ast_csr_ctm_aliases`).
* The bridge forwards the request's size field as `AWSIZE` and `ARSIZE`
  without a clamp when the field is two bits wide, so a 32-bit bridge issues
  an eight-byte transfer size on request (ARM IHI 0022 A3.4.1); the shared
  AXI checker's `ast_axi_ar_size_legal` fails without
  `asm_j2a_size_within_bus`, which holds the host to the bus width in the
  `jtag2axi` environment.

The proofs and cover traces add seven points the chapters do not state.

* The STAP chain takes its scan control from the OR of the PTAP's IR and DR
  controls, without the PTAP 3DCR select or the instruction in the term, so
  every scan clocks the STAP SIB bits with the bits that leave the PTAP's
  register and an open SIB's 3DCR with the next scan. The cover trace that
  selects the SEP STAP loads no instruction at all: under IDCODE, a three-bit
  DR scan opens the SEP SIB and a four-bit scan sets its `stap_sel`, so a
  downstream TAP's TMS follows the host without TAP_3DCR ever being loaded.
  The T4 covers are reached at step 41 through this route
  (<<dtp-formal-depths>>).
* The bypass register's select term shares its default arm with the
  undefined opcodes, so IDCODE, INV_BYPASS and SELECT_IJTAG scans clock the
  bypass register too while TDO reads their own register
  (`ast_tdr_select_one_hot`).
* The TMP status register resets to `2'b10` with the bypass-escape bit at
  bit 0, so the escape exit is disabled after reset and needs a TMP_STATUS
  write first (`cov_tmp_off_by_escape`).
* The network's address map lists each window's end address as its last
  byte while the crossbar decoder treats the end address as exclusive, so
  the last byte address of every window (`0x1FF`, `0x20F` and so on to
  `0x2FF`) completes with DECERR; word-aligned accesses never reach it
  (`ast_csr_unmapped_decerr`).
* `ct_dst` lasts two cycles instead of one when the synchronized request
  drops in the cycle after the acknowledge rose: the receiver keeps the pulse
  through its transition to the wait state (`ast_hs_dst_is_one_pulse`).
* A series-control write with the reserved op code `2'b11` programs a series
  whose requests the machine issues as writes (`cov_j2a_disable_in_state`
  traces reach the write states through it).
* The instruction decode is `jtag_instruction_decoded_e'(2 ** shift)`. The
  open-path frontend sizes the operand of a type cast on its own, so the
  power is 32 bits wide in the model and an opcode above `0x1E` decodes to
  zero or to a sign-extended pattern, while Verilator sizes the operand to
  the cast type and decodes every opcode. The T2 module therefore assumes an
  opcode at or below `0x1E` at every Update-IR (`asm_ir_open_path_decode_range`)
  and covers that half of the instruction space; the zero-length bypass, the
  upper capability registers and the SEP and SMC bridge instructions have no
  open-path cover. The instruction package's own enum values use the shift
  form `DECODED_IR_WIDTH'(1'b1) << opcode`, which every frontend sizes alike
  (`formal-property-style.adoc`, Accepted constructs).

Two reset paths are checks for the licensed X-propagation app
(<<dtp-formal-apps>>): `cla_clock_stop_o` is combinational with no reset, and
the `cla_clock_stop` synchronizer inside `jtag_debug_ctrl_reg` has its reset
tied high.

[[dtp-formal-assumptions]]
==== Assumptions and environments

Every top has one environment module bound at the top, in the shape of
`formal/fpv/sby/dtp_sby_env.sv`: every asynchronous reset is asserted up to
the first sampling edge, so each clock domain starts from its reset state,
and released there for the rest of the trace
(`asm_env_resets_asserted`, `asm_env_resets_released`). Each property module
adds `asm_initial_reset` from the macro layer on its own reset. Beyond that:

* `dtp`: `asm_env_scan_reset_inactive` holds the scan reset high.
  `dbg_disable_i`, `tms`, `tdi` and every host scan input are free, except
  that the return data of the external scan chains and of the downstream
  TAPs holds through the tck-high phase
  (`asm_env_scan_returns_hold_while_tck_high`), the shape of a JTAG slave's
  TDO. The AXI responder ports are free too: the JTAG-domain targets never
  reach them. The T1 item binds the shared JTAG checker `ocah_jtag_fv` on
  the primary TAP (`dtp_jtag_fv_bind.sv`), which assumes the host's phase
  rule, `asm_jtag_tms_tdi_stable_while_tck_high`, and asserts the TAP's rules
  (<<dtp-formal-properties>>); that item also reads `dtp_tap_sby_env.sv`,
  whose `asm_env_ir_open_path_decode_range` bounds the opcode an Update-IR
  loads to `0x1E`, as the T2 module does for the frontend sizing recorded in
  <<dtp-formal-observations>>: a wider opcode sets several instruction bits
  in the model, among them the zero-length bypass that routes TDO around its
  retimer. The T2 module adds
  `asm_ir_open_path_decode_range`, an opcode at or below `0x1E` at every
  Update-IR, for the frontend sizing recorded in <<dtp-formal-observations>>.
* `jtag2axi`, elaborated in the shape of the OTP AXI-Lite bridges (32-bit
  address and data, pipeline depth 3): the JTAG-side controls are free inputs
  constrained to the TAP's shape, `asm_j2a_scan_ctrl_exclusive` (capture,
  shift and update never overlap), `asm_j2a_update_follows_shift_or_capture`
  (an update is one cycle and closes a scan a capture opened),
  `asm_j2a_select_one_hot` (at most one bridge select), and
  `asm_j2a_controls_tck_synchronous` (the disable and the update enable hold
  the value the following posedge samples across the negedge, the shape of
  the DTP's synchronizer and TAP flags). Two cutpoints in the task file keep
  the target on the control. The shared shift register is free at every step,
  so an Update-DR presents any request without the seventy shift cycles that
  loading it through TDI costs. In the `bmc` and `cover` tasks the CDC's
  response side toward the request machine (`src_resp`) is free too, so a
  response needs no round trip through the clearable CDC, whose clear
  handshake after reset holds every channel for more than sixty steps before
  the first request can cross; the `prove` task keeps the CDC in the loop for
  the property-directed engine. The shared AXI checker `ocah_axi_fv`, bound
  by `dtp_jtag2axi_axi_fv_bind.sv` on the CDC's response side toward the
  request machine, constrains that responder in every task with its slave
  rules assumed: a B or R beat arrives only with a request outstanding
  (`asm_axi_b_not_before_aw`, `asm_axi_r_not_before_ar`), a valid holds
  with its payload until its ready (`asm_axi_b_valid_held`,
  `asm_axi_r_valid_held`, `asm_axi_b_payload_stable`,
  `asm_axi_r_payload_stable`) and a valid is low in reset; the real CDC
  satisfies them, and `ast_j2a_resp_only_while_waiting` is the T7 property
  that holds only under them. The same instance asserts the request
  machine's master rules (<<dtp-formal-properties>>). The AXI ports lie
  behind the CDC, whose clear handshake after reset keeps them idle within
  the bounded depth, so they carry no checker instance. The
  environment module adds what the protocol leaves open:
  `asm_j2a_src_r_single_beat` and `asm_j2a_r_single_beat`, since every
  request is a single beat, and `asm_j2a_size_within_bus`, since the request's
  size field admits a transfer wider than a 32-bit bus and the bridge forwards
  it unchanged (<<dtp-formal-observations>>). Every proof over the control
  logic holds for the real signals because the free ones include every value
  they can take.
* `cross_trigger_network`: the AXI-Lite manager is the DTP's bridge, so
  `asm_axil_one_outstanding` allows one write and one read in flight, and in
  the T9 item the shared AXI checker bound by `dtp_ctn_axi_fv_bind.sv` with
  its master rules assumed holds each valid with its payload until its ready
  (`asm_axi_aw_valid_held`, `asm_axi_w_valid_held`, `asm_axi_ar_valid_held`,
  `asm_axi_aw_payload_stable`, `asm_axi_w_payload_stable`,
  `asm_axi_ar_payload_stable`) and keeps each valid low in reset; the same
  instance asserts the network's responses (<<dtp-formal-properties>>). The
  T6 and T8 items on this top read the port through its covers alone and
  take no handshake assumption. `asm_ctp_peer_four_phase` models the far end
  of external port 0 on
  the synchronized, de-inverted request and acknowledge (the far end
  acknowledges a request it sees and withdraws the acknowledge after the
  request drops, holds its own request until it sees the acknowledge, and
  raises no new one while the acknowledge is high); `asm_ctp_stretch_small`
  bounds port 0's `STRETCH_MULT` to 4. The pad inputs are otherwise free.

Every `cov_` label stays reachable under these assumptions; an unreached
cover fails the item, and an assumption change re-runs every cover of its
top (`formal-property-style.adoc`, cover reachability).

[[dtp-formal-depths]]
==== Tasks and depths

Each block has its own `bmc`, `cover` and `prove` task triple, named
`<block>_bmc`, `<block>_cover` and `<block>_prove`; the T8 item runs the
first two (<<dtp-formal-depths>>). On the `dtp` top the
triples share `formal/fpv/sby/dtp.sby`, selected by task tags, and one app
table per block passes its triple in `args`, so `testlists/formal.toml` lists
one item per block. The `jtag2axi` and `cross_trigger_network` tops have their
own task files. The `prove` task carries no depth; `bmc` and `cover` share
one depth per block, set above the step at which the block's deepest cover
is reached. The table gives the step at which each block's deepest cover
is reachable and the depth set above it; the derivations that follow give
the structural latency of each route.

The bound follows the block's structural latency. Under `multiclock on` every
step is a time point and one `tck` cycle costs two steps; the reset
environment releases the resets at the first `tck` posedge, so a state that is
`n` `tck` edges from Test-Logic-Reset is first observable at step `3 + 2n`.
`clk_i` is a second explicit clock on the `dtp` top and toggles freely, so a
`clk_i`-domain latency of `k` cycles adds steps only where `2k` exceeds the
`tck` path. The `cross_trigger_network` top is single-clock and its depth is
in `clk_i` cycles. Two `tck` edge counts recur: an instruction load from
Test-Logic-Reset is 13 edges (Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Select-IR,
Capture-IR, Shift-IR, six shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-IR,
Update-IR), and a DR scan of `b` bits is `b + 4` edges (Select-DR,
Capture-DR, Shift-DR, `b` shifts with the last leaving to Exit1-DR,
Update-DR).

[cols="1,4,1,1",options="header",]
|===
|Target |Deepest cover and the route the trace takes |Step |Depth
|T1 |`cov_tap_tlr_from_five_tms_high`: Run-Test/Idle, Select-DR, Capture-DR, then five TMS-high edges into Test-Logic-Reset |19 |30
|T2 |`cov_undefined_opcode_bypass`: the undefined opcode `0x0F` loaded, then a DR scan reaches Shift-DR through the bypass register; the thirty-one opcode covers each load one instruction |33 |40
|T3 |`cov_tmp_off_by_escape`: TMP_STATUS loaded and its escape bit written, CLAMP_HOLD loaded, BYPASS_ALT loaded with one shift, persistence off at the edge after Update-IR |67 |75
|T4 |`cov_stap_disabled_while_selected` on the extra STAP: two IDCODE scans of three and four bits open the SIB and set `stap_sel` through the chain's IR-and-DR scan control, then the synchronized disable rises (<<dtp-formal-observations>>) |41 |55
|T5 |`cov_sib_disabled_while_open` on the DFD SIB and the eight SIB patterns: SELECT_IJTAG loaded, a 3-bit DR scan writes the SIBs, the disable rises two edges later |41 |55
|T6 |`cov_dbgctl_boot_stall_ovrd_stall_high`: DEBUG_CONTROL loaded, a 5-bit DR scan sets both bits |37 |55 on `dtp`; the `ctn_clkstop_fpv` covers are reached at step 6 and its triple uses depth 10
|T7 |`cov_j2a_series_reads_admitted`: a CTRL update programs a read series and `FIFO_DEPTH + 1` data updates are admitted; a single write or read completes through the free response side at step 13 |23 |40; the `prove` task runs `abc pdr` on the model with the CDC in the loop, since k-induction cannot close `$past` relations under explicit clocks, where an induction window may hold a single `tck` edge
|T8 |`cov_hs_four_phase_complete` and `cov_stretch_max_width`: a CSR write sets the mode or the stretch, a source pulse starts the sender, the peer acknowledges and releases |10 |20; the item runs `bmc` and `cover` alone, since the block is P1 and signs off on a bounded result
|T9 |`cov_csr_ctp_read_after_write`: a write to CONFIG and a read of it complete through the crossbar and the register block |6 |12; the `prove` task also carries the shared AXI checker's assertions on the port
|===

A cover added at a greater distance raises its block's depth. The
derivations behind the table: an instruction load from Test-Logic-Reset is
13 `tck` edges and a DR scan of `b` bits is `b + 4`, so the T2 and T3 routes
above cost 17 and about 33 edges; the T4 route through the chain's scan control costs 19 edges
(<<dtp-formal-observations>>); T5 and T6
cost about 24 edges; the T7 machine completes a request in about five `tck`
cycles once its response side is free, while with the CDC in the loop the
clear handshake after reset lets nothing cross within sixty steps
(<<dtp-formal-assumptions>>); the CTN routes are single-clock cycle counts. The T1
depth also follows a diameter-plus-reset argument: depth 30 is thirteen
`tck` edges after reset release, the state-graph diameter of seven plus a
five-edge TMS reset sequence with one edge of margin.

[[dtp-formal-apps]]
==== App checks and path assignment

[cols="2,2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Check |Path |Targets |What it establishes
|Property checking (`fpv`) |Open, `sby` |T1 to T9 as listed |The properties above, with `prove` for the P0 set and bounded `bmc` plus `cover` at the block depth.
|Connectivity |Licensed connectivity app, companion-owned config |T4, T5 and the static wiring of `dtp`: the STAP chain order I/O, SMC, SEP, extra, return; each `dbg_disable_i` field to exactly its path (the eleven fields of the simulation bench's 22-cell contract); the IC_RESET slice packing SMC, SEP, EXT to `reset_hold`; the pad-ring tie-offs on the unused CTP directions |Point-to-point wiring the property style cannot express as a single boolean without naming every net; on the open path the tie-offs and the chain order are constant assertions inside T4 and T5.
|CSR access policy from the register description |Licensed register app driven by the SystemRDL under `hw/ip/cross_trigger/*/regs/` |T9 |Every field's access, reset value and side effect for the sixteen CTPs and the CTM; the open path carries the address map and one slice.
|Security-path checks for the debug-disable gating |Open, `sby`, the P0 set of T4, T5 and T7 |T4, T5, T7 |With a disable field high, its path's select, TDO, host enables and bridge machine are quiet, and a release replays nothing; unbounded by `prove`.
|Reset X-safety |Licensed X-propagation app |T6, T7 |The two unreset paths named in <<dtp-formal-observations>>, and the JTAG2AXI CDC after an ACLK reset mid-transaction.
|Unreachability |Licensed unreachability app over the merged simulation coverage database |The bridge reset-timing, CDC, and long-wait bins |Whether the reset-timing and CDC clear/isolate bins and the long-wait poll bins in `cov/config/verilator/coverage_policy.toml` are unreachable by construction; an unreachable bin is dispositioned as such in the policy file.
|===

The licensed apps run through the same runner from the companion's formal
configuration, named by the site layer (`run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification);
their scripts read the same property files. The open tree carries no licensed
collateral.

[[dtp-formal-running]]
==== Running and grading

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal                    # the smoke group: dtp_tap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items fpv        # every formal item
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --items dtp_stap_fpv
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal --dry-run          # the rendered sby command
----

The `flist` stage regenerates `formal/build/dtp.f` from Bender, the filelist
all three task files read; the `formal` stage runs one item's `bmc`, `cover`
and `prove` tasks in one `sby` invocation whose work directories are
`<run>/stages/formal/<item>_<block>_<task>/`, for example
`dtp_tap_fpv_tap_bmc`. The `smoke` group holds `dtp_tap_fpv`; the `fpv` group
holds every item: `dtp_tap_fpv`, `dtp_ir_fpv`, `dtp_reset_fpv`,
`dtp_stap_fpv`, `dtp_sib_fpv` and `dtp_dbgctl_fpv` on the `dtp` top,
`jtag2axi_ctrl_fpv` on `jtag2axi`, and `ctn_clkstop_fpv`, `ctn_xtrig_fpv`
and `ctn_csr_fpv` on `cross_trigger_network`. The runner grades an item from
the task status files and JUnit reports, never from the exit code: every
task `PASS` with every cover reached is `PASS`; a counterexample, an
induction failure or an unreached cover is `FAIL` in the `formal_fail`
bucket, and `result.json` carries the `formal` object with the proven and
covered counts (`run-dv.adoc`, Formal result grading).

The FAIL path is checked by inverting one assertion body, for example
`dr_select_i == ir_select_i` in `ast_tap_select_exclusive`, and running the
same command: the `bmc` task reports `FAIL` with the counterexample trace at
`stages/formal/dtp_tap_fpv_tap_bmc/engine_0/trace.vcd`, the `prove` task
reports `FAIL`, and the item is `FAIL`. Every item's FAIL path is checked
the same way.

The open path needs a yosys-slang build with the concurrent-assertion
lowering and an SMT solver beside `sby` (`formal-property-style.adoc`,
Open-path toolchain); the runner guide's Formal verification chapter covers
the local and container runs and the licensed backends. Formal mode is a
developer-host flow: hosted CI runs no formal job.

[[dtp-formal-dependencies]]
==== Dependencies between targets

T3 and T4 hold the P0 set and stand alone on the `dtp` top. T7 and T9 take
the AXI and AXI-Lite handshake rules from the shared checker `ocah_axi_fv`,
bound with the environment's side assumed, and T1 takes the host's phase
rule from `ocah_jtag_fv`; the point-to-point peer model stays in the
`cross_trigger_network` environment, which T8 needs. The
licensed connectivity, register, X-propagation and unreachability apps read
the same property files from the companion's formal configuration. A target
is complete when its item passes with every cover reached and its FAIL path
is checked.

=== Regression Groups

The simulation groups below come from `testlists/all.toml`;
`testlists/formal.toml` carries the formal item `dtp_tap_fpv` in its own
`smoke` group (<<dtp-formal-plan>>).

[cols=",,",options="header",]
|===
|Group |Tests |Purpose
|`smoke` |`dtp_sanity_test`, `dtp_jtag_idcode_test` |Fast TAP bring-up gate
|`functional` |Smoke plus the JTAG2AXI `single_write` and `single_write_read` scenarios |Fast functional regression
|`basic_jtag` |The Smoke and Basic JTAG scenarios |TAP, instruction, reset, BSR-loopback, and TMP smoke coverage
|`debug_tdr` |The TMP_STATUS, IC_RESET, DEBUG_CONTROL, and CAPS tests |Debug TDR coverage
|`all` |Every scenario-expanded test (`expected_count`-pinned in `testlists/all.toml`) |Full OCAH DTP regression
|`jtag2axi` |All JTAG2AXI write/read/error/robustness scenarios |AXI/AXI-Lite bridge coverage
|`scan` |STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG scenarios |IEEE 1838 and IEEE 1687 coverage
|`dbg_disable` |The two debug-disable matrix tests plus every resource-specific directed gating test |Per-field `dbg_disable_t` closure (22-cell coverage contract)
|`xtrig` |XTRIG, CTP, and CTM scenarios |Cross-trigger and CSR coverage
|===

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-package]]
=== Signoff Package

The DTP signoff package is the set of records below, each owned by one
document of this book or by the tracker. The book carries the plans,
contracts, dispositions, and scope exclusions, which hold across candidates; the
tracker's signoff item carries what belongs to one candidate: the commit,
the run trees it was qualified with, the open issues and their release
dispositions, and the release approvals.

[cols="1,3",options="header",]
|===
|Record |Where it lives
|Verification scope and requirements traceability |`DTP_SCOPE_TRACEABILITY.adoc`: the source of truth per question, the F1 to F5 matrix, its credit rules, and the Credit column
|Testbench and VIP architecture |`DTP_TB_ARCH.adoc`: the unified environment, the DUT-local model ownership, the supported simulator matrix, and the realization differences
|Component closures |<<dtp-vplan-signoff>>: one subsection per plan section with its evidence contract, negative hooks, dispositions, and coverage review, and the *Evidence* field of every procedure
|Coverage targets and closure |`DTP_FCOV.adoc`: the signoff target per metric, the evidence paths, and the closure policy that binds `cov/config/verilator/coverage_policy.toml`
|Regression qualification |<<dtp-vplan-qualification>>: the supported pairs, the qualification run and its grading, the retained evidence, and the non-passing-leaf disposition rule
|Test quality and scope exclusions |<<dtp-vplan-signoff-quality>>: the per-family quality review, the fail-closed evidence, and the bench scope exclusions
|Known limitations |<<dtp-vplan-known-limitations>>
|Candidate commit, run trees, open issues, approvals |The tracker's signoff item and the release record; the runner's `result.json` names the commit and the tool versions of every retained run
|===

[[dtp-vplan-qualification]]
=== Release Qualification

A release candidate is qualified by regression evidence on the supported
framework and simulator pairs, graded by the runner's leaf rules and
retained with the run tree; a passing set of individual tests is not a
qualification. The rules below bind the DTP bench; the runner's grading,
record, and CI contracts are in `tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`.

[cols="1,1,2,3",options="header",]
|===
|Framework |Simulator |Where it runs |Qualification evidence
|cocotb (PyUVM) |Verilator, the version the CI toolchain step builds and the runner records in `result.json` |Hosted CI: `smoke` on every pull request and push to `main`, `all` at one seed nightly, `all` at three seeds with coverage weekly; a developer host for the same commands |The weekly run tree of the candidate commit, or a developer-host run of the same selection
|cocotb (PyUVM) |VCS |A licensed host |A full `all` run at the candidate commit, with `--cov` for the commercial metric set
|SV-UVM |VCS |A licensed host |A full `all` run at the candidate commit, with `--cov`; every canonical scenario has an SV-UVM realization, so the selection is the same scenario inventory
|Formal (SymbiYosys) |`dtp_tap_fpv` and the `fpv` group of `testlists/formal.toml` |A developer host; hosted CI runs no formal job |Corroboration of the rows the formal plan names, never regression credit
|===

A framework and simulator pair the runner lists but this table omits is
not a release pair. The hosted nightly is a triage signal; the qualification
run is the weekly tier or a run on a developer or licensed host.

==== The qualification run

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items all --regress --reseed 3 --cov --sim-jobs <n>
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items all --regress --reseed 3 --cov --tool vcs --sim-jobs <n>
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items all --regress --reseed 3 --cov --sim-jobs <n>
----

The run is qualified when all of the following hold in its run-level
`result.json` and `stages/regress/regression.json`:

* the planned leaves number the `all` group's `expected_count` times the
  seed count, `tests.completed` is `true`, and `progress.missing` and
  `progress.interrupted` are empty, so no leaf was skipped, cancelled, or
  cut off by a budget;
* every leaf's final attempt is `PASS` and the run status is `PASS`, with
  the exceptions the disposition rule below allows; `ERROR`,
  `TIMEOUT`, and `UNKNOWN` are infrastructure verdicts and never stand in
  for a design result, so a leaf in one of them is rerun with its recorded
  seed on the qualification host before the run is judged;
* with coverage, the `cov_report` record is `PASS` and the effective
  figures meet the targets of `DTP_FCOV.adoc`, or every hole below them has
  a disposition in the policy file.

A leaf that passes only after a `--retry` attempt is a flake: the runner
lists it under `flaky_tests` with its failing attempt and rerun command,
it counts as passing, and the qualification report names every flake with
its first failure reason. Hosted CI runs no retry, so a flake there shows as
seeds of one test disagreeing within the run, which the weekly digest lists.

==== Retained evidence

The candidate is the commit `result.json` records in its git block; the
run tree of every qualification run is kept as a whole: the run-level
`result.json` (status, tool versions, seeds, the coverage summary),
`stages/regress/regression.json` (every attempt, the rerun command of every
failed and flaky leaf), each leaf's `result.json`, `logs/`, and
`results/results.xml`, and `cov/report/` with its `summary.json` and
`coverage-details.json`. The hosted tiers publish the same tree as the
`dv-run-dtp-verilator-<tier>` artifact, with the per-leaf coverage
databases excluded after the merge; the retention periods are the CI
contract's. A qualification run on a developer or licensed host is copied
into the release record.

==== Non-passing leaves

Every leaf whose final attempt is not `PASS` carries one disposition in the
release record: a design defect the release ships with, an infrastructure
verdict cleared by the rerun above, or a release blocker. A run whose only
non-passing leaves carry a release-record disposition is judged on the
remaining leaves.

A release that ships without a qualifying run on one of the licensed
pairs records the deferral, the pair, and the last qualifying commit in
the release record; the documentation carries no such record.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff]]
=== Component Signoff

One subsection per plan section. Each records the evidence contract its
scenarios meet in both realizations, the negative-validation hooks that must
fail a run, the disposition of every checker-table row whose realization
differs from its plan text, and the functional-coverage points the group
fires. Feature credit stays with `DTP_SCOPE_TRACEABILITY.adoc`; the group
sizes are the `expected_count` guards in `testlists/all.toml`.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag]]
==== Primary JTAG and boundary scan (section 0)

The section 0 scenarios are the `basic_jtag` group and realize the F1
rows of the scope matrix: the IEEE 1149.1 state machine and its resets,
instruction decode and the BYPASS family, IDCODE, the boundary-scan
instructions against the bench's compact loopback, RUNBIST, and the two
test-mode-persistence clamp instructions. `smoke` carries `dtp_sanity_test`
and `dtp_jtag_idcode_test` as the PR gate; `all` carries the group into the
nightly and weekly regressions. The formal item `dtp_tap_fpv`
(`testlists/formal.toml`, target T1) proves the TAP controller's one-hot
state, the five-TMS reset, and the legal transitions where SymbiYosys is
installed (`run_dv.py --dut dtp --mode formal`); it corroborates F1.1 and
F1.2 and is not regression credit.

Every scenario runs at least sixteen seeded passes. A pass records named
`CHK-*` evidence through the shared JTAG checker and finalizes it once; a
pass with zero checks or a missing required ID fails. The passive pin-level
scan monitor cross-checks every IR and DR scan the sequence issues
(`CHK-SCAN-COUNT`, `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN`, `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN`), and `CHK-NONVAC`
records that the pass compared something. On SV-UVM the always-on
`dtp_scoreboard` features `ir_decode`, `idcode`, and `bypass` end each run
as `CHK-SB-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-SB-IDCODE`, and `CHK-SB-BYPASS`, and the
`dtp_tap_fsm_checker` ends it as `CHK-TAP-STATE`. Each procedure's
*Evidence* field names the records it lands.

[cols="1,2,3,2,1",options="header",]
|===
|Row |Tests |Named evidence |Coverage points |Credit
|F1.1 TAP state machine |`dtp_sanity_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-TAP-STATE`, `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-TMS5`, `CHK-TAP-GOTO` required; `bypass` and `idcode` scoreboard features. cocotb: the state-visit and transition checks are plain assertions of the sequence (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions>>) |`c_state_*` (the sixteen states), `c_arc_*` (the thirty-two legal arcs) |YES
|F1.2 TAP reset |`dtp_jtag_trst_test`, `dtp_jtag_trst_por_independence_test`, `dtp_jtag_tlr_reset_test` |`CHK-TAP-TRST-ASYNC`, `CHK-TAP-TRST-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-POR-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-TMS5`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-IDCODE`, `CHK-TLR-TDR-DEFAULT`, `CHK-TLR-RESUME`, `CHK-IDCODE-RAW`, `CHK-IDCODE-RECOVERY`, `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-STATE` |`c_smoke_reset_source_trst`, `c_smoke_reset_source_tms_to_tlr`, `c_reset_trst_from_active`, `c_reset_tms5_from_active` |YES
|F1.3 IDCODE |`dtp_jtag_idcode_test` |`CHK-IDCODE-MARKER`, `CHK-IDCODE-MANUFACTURER`, `CHK-IDCODE-PART-NUMBER`, `CHK-IDCODE-VERSION`, `CHK-IDCODE-RAW`, `CHK-IDCODE-STABLE`; `idcode` scoreboard feature |`c_smoke_idcode_default_after_reset`, `c_smoke_idcode_explicit_instruction`, `c_smoke_idcode_marker_1` |YES
|F1.4 Instruction decode and BYPASS |`dtp_jtag_bypass_test`, `dtp_jtag_inv_bypass_test`, `dtp_jtag_zero_length_bypass_test`, `dtp_jtag_undef_instr_test` |`CHK-BYPASS-00`, `CHK-BYPASS-3F`, `CHK-BYPASS-LATENCY`, `CHK-BYPASS-DELAY`, `CHK-INV-BYPASS`, `CHK-ZLB-PASSTHROUGH`, `CHK-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-UNDEF-NO-SELECT`, `CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` (the one-bit and zero-bit chain lengths); `ir_decode` and `bypass` scoreboard features |`c_ir_opcode_*` (all sixty-four encodings), `c_ir_category_*`, `c_ir_behavior_enabled_instruction`, `c_ir_behavior_undefined_to_bypass` |YES
|F1.5 Boundary-scan instructions |`dtp_jtag_sample_preload_test`, `dtp_jtag_extest_test`, `dtp_jtag_intest_test`, `dtp_jtag_clamp_test`, `dtp_jtag_highz_test`, `dtp_jtag_ac_extest_train_test`, `dtp_jtag_ac_extest_pulse_test` |`CHK-BSR-LOOPBACK`, `CHK-BSR-SELECT`, `CHK-BSR-SCAN-CTRL`, `CHK-BSR-RTI` (the EXTEST_TRAIN launch strobe), `CHK-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-BYPASS-DELAY` (the CLAMP and HIGHZ bypass path), the scan cross-check IDs |The seven instructions' `c_ir_opcode_*` points; `cg_scan_boundary` on the commercial simulators |YES against the compact identity loopback (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions>>)
|F1.6 RUNBIST and the TMP clamps |`dtp_jtag_runbist_test`, `dtp_jtag_clamp_hold_test`, `dtp_jtag_clamp_release_test` |`CHK-RUNBIST-RESPONSE`, `CHK-RUNBIST-DR-LEN`, `CHK-DFT-RUNBIST`, `CHK-DFT-SIB-SELECT`, `CHK-DFT-SCAN-CTRL`, `CHK-TMP-PERSIST`, `CHK-BYPASS-DELAY`, `CHK-IR-DECODE`, `CHK-BSR-LOOPBACK` |The three instructions' `c_ir_opcode_*` points; `c_tmp_persistence_*` in `dtp_debug_tdr_fcov` |YES
|===

The instruction encodings, TAP-state encodings, and IDCODE fields the checks
compare against are transcriptions of the `jtag_intf_unit` and `jtag_ptap`
documentation, each table naming the document and section it copies
(`cocotb/env/dtp_types.py`). The responder memory and the design collateral
are the only other sources of expected values; nothing is derived from the
observation under check.

Two hooks prove that the evidence path gates the verdict. Each is an
environment variable on cocotb and a plusarg of the same name on SV-UVM, and
a run with either set must fail:

* `DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` corrupts every family expectation
  (loopback, bypass delay, decoded instruction, TMP persistence) in every
  scenario that attaches the family checker.
* `DTP_JTAG_TAP_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` arms a wrong IDCODE and a wrong TAP-state
  prediction in the reset-family scenarios and, on SV-UVM, in the sanity
  scenario's TAP checker.

[source,bash]
----
DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag_extest_test
DTP_JTAG_TAP_CHECKER_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag_tlr_reset_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_sanity_test --plusarg +DTP_JTAG_TAP_CHECKER_NEGATIVE
----

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions]]
===== Plan-to-realization dispositions

Each row is a section 0 checker whose realization differs from its plan
text, with where the behavior is proven and the scope of any exclusion.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Realization |Disposition
|`sanity_fsm_visit_chk`, `sanity_bypass_latency_chk`, `sanity_scan_path_chk` |cocotb: plain assertions of the sequence with no attached checker, so `DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` does not reach them and any mismatch fails the pass directly. SV-UVM: `CHK-TAP-STATE`, `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR`, `CHK-TAP-TLR-TMS5`, `CHK-TAP-GOTO`, `CHK-BYPASS-LATENCY`, and the `CHK-IDCODE-*` records through the env checker |Named evidence on the SV-UVM twin; the cocotb sequence records no named evidence
|`extest_output_drive_chk`, `extest_capture_chk`, `intest_input_drive_chk`, `intest_capture_chk`, `clamp_output_hold_chk`, `clamp_glitch_free_chk`, `highz_oen_deassert_chk`, `highz_recover_chk`, `sample_capture_chk`, `preload_no_drive_chk`, `preload_retain_chk`, `extest_train_pattern_chk`, `extest_train_no_disturb_chk`, `extest_pulse_single_chk`, `extest_pulse_timing_chk` |The bench models no boundary cells: the BSR is the compact identity loopback of `DtpScanModel` (TB_ARCH, DUT-local model ownership). The scenarios prove instruction decode, the chain select and the TAP's capture/shift/update strobes across each scan (`CHK-BSR-SELECT`, `CHK-BSR-SCAN-CTRL`), the Run-Test/Idle launch strobe of EXTEST_TRAIN (`CHK-BSR-RTI`), and capture and shift through the loopback (`CHK-BSR-LOOPBACK`), plus the bypass path of CLAMP and HIGHZ; pin drive, output enables, and glitch-free transitions have no observable on this bench, and each row's text states the protocol evidence that stands in for them |Out of bench scope: boundary-cell pin behavior belongs to the integrating design's cells; the DTP carries instruction decode, chain select, and the scan strobes, which these fifteen rows credit through `CHK-BSR-*`
|`inv_bypass_no_side_effect_chk` |No direct check; the `ir_decode` scoreboard feature and the bypass reference model bound what INV_BYPASS selects |Credited through the scoreboard features
|===

===== Coverage review

The `basic_jtag` group owns every `c_state_*` point, every
`c_arc_*` point, both `c_reset_*` reachability points, all five `c_smoke_*`
points, and the `c_ir_behavior_enabled_instruction` and
`c_ir_behavior_undefined_to_bypass` points of `dtp_fcov`. Of the sixty-four
`c_ir_opcode_*` points it owns the IEEE 1149.1 and IEEE 1838 instructions,
RUNBIST, the two clamp instructions, TMP_STATUS, and every reserved and
undefined encoding. The encodings it does not own are IC_RESET, TAP_3DCR,
DEBUG_CONTROL, JTAG_CAPS, SELECT_IJTAG, and the JTAG2AXI capability and
transfer TDRs, together with `c_ir_category_ijtag`,
`c_ir_category_jtag2axi`, and `c_ir_behavior_disabled_to_bypass`; those are
the instructions of F2 through F5 and belong to the `debug_tdr`, `scan`,
`jtag2axi`, and `dbg_disable` groups. On the commercial simulators the
`cg_tap_state`, `cg_tap_arc`, and `cg_jtag_instruction` covergroups carry
the same bins and `cg_scan_boundary` adds the loopback observations.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-dbg]]
==== TMP, debug control, IC_RESET, and capabilities (section 1)

The section 1 scenarios are the `debug_tdr` group and realize the F2 rows
of the scope matrix: the TMP_STATUS register and the persistence state
behind it, the IC_RESET overrides and their hold across Test-Logic-Reset,
the DEBUG_CONTROL clock-stop and boot-stall fields with their pin
observables, and the four read-only capability TDRs. The two CLA clock-stop
scenarios also carry F5.7, the clock-stop aggregation of the cross-trigger
network. The formal targets T3 (reset hierarchy and persistence), T6 (debug
control and clock stop), and T2 (the read-only IDCODE and CAPS captures)
corroborate these rows where SymbiYosys is installed and are not regression
credit.

Every scenario runs at least sixteen seeded passes. On SV-UVM each pass
attaches the family checker with the scenario's required IDs and records
every comparison through it: `CHK-DBG-TDR` is a decoded TDR field or a whole
TDR against its expected value, `CHK-DBG-PIN` is a pin observable read
through `dtp_tb_if`, polled across the two-flop synchronizer and the output
flop where the observable is `stop_clks`, `CHK-CAPS` and `CHK-CAPS-RO` are
the capability value and its readback after a write attempt, and
`CHK-TMP-PERSIST` and `CHK-TMP-ESCAPE` are the two TMP_STATUS bits. The
scan cross-check adds `CHK-SCAN-COUNT`, `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN`, and
`CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` for every IR and DR scan the sequence issues and
`CHK-NONVAC` for the pass; the always-on `dtp_scoreboard` ends every run
with the `ir_decode` feature as `CHK-SB-IR-DECODE`, and the env TAP checker
ends it as `CHK-TAP-STATE`. A pass with zero checks or a missing required ID
fails at finalization. On cocotb the same sequences compare through the base
sequence's `assert_equal`: each comparison is one `CHECK <name> expected=
observed=` log line and a Python assertion that fails the pass at once, no
family checker is attached, and no `CHK-*` record or `CHECKER_SUMMARY` is
written (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-dbg-dispositions>>). Each procedure's
*Evidence* field names the records its SV-UVM realization lands.

The expected values are transcriptions of the design documentation: the
TDR layouts and bit positions from the `jtag_ptap` architecture chapter (the
"JTAG Capabilities" and "IC_RESET Support" tables) and the interface-unit
instruction table, the JTAG_CAPS field values from the elaboration
parameters of the standalone DTP, the CAPS geometry from the DV-owned bridge
geometry table that the JTAG2AXI geometry gate also uses, and the pin
defaults from the reset values of the DEBUG_CONTROL and IC_RESET registers.
The clock-stop expectation is the OR of the programmed `jtag_clock_stop`
bit and the request vector the sequence drives; nothing is derived from the
observation under check.

[cols="1,2,3,2,1",options="header",]
|===
|Row |Tests |Named evidence |Coverage points |Credit
|F2.1 TMP_STATUS: reset value, persistence across chip reset, BYPASS escape |`dtp_jtag_tmp_status_register_smoke_test`, `dtp_jtag_tmp_status_chrst_n_in_persistence_test`, `dtp_jtag_tmp_status_bypass_escape_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-TMP-PERSIST`, `CHK-TMP-ESCAPE`, `CHK-DBG-TDR`, `CHK-BYPASS-DELAY`, `CHK-IR-DECODE`; the `idcode` and `bypass` scoreboard features. cocotb: plain assertions |`c_tmp_persistence_reset_off`, `c_tmp_persistence_clamp_hold_on`, `c_tmp_persistence_chip_reset_preserved`, `c_tmp_persistence_bypass_escape_release`, `c_tmp_read_value_*`, `c_tmp_escape_*` |YES; the named evidence is the SV-UVM twin's
|F2.2 IC_RESET: per-port `reset_enable` and `reset_control`, `reset_hold` across Test-Logic-Reset, TRST restores the defaults |`dtp_jtag_ic_reset_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-DBG-TDR` (the register after each reset), `CHK-DBG-PIN` (`jtag_ic_reset_<slice>_ovrd`, `jtag_ic_reset_<slice>_ctrl_n`). cocotb: plain assertions |`c_ic_reset_slice_{smc,sep,ext}`, `c_ic_reset_override_{disabled,enabled}`, `c_ic_reset_{asserted,deasserted}`, `c_ic_reset_hold_{tlr_preserve,tlr_restore,trst_restore}` |YES for the standalone three-slice instantiation; the SMU-composed external slice is OUT
|F2.3 DEBUG_CONTROL clock stop: `stop_clks` from `jtag_clock_stop` OR any request bit, `cla_clock_stop` readback from the requests alone, `cla_clock_stop_en` exported |`dtp_dbg_ctrl_clk_stop_jtag_clock_stop_test`, `dtp_dbg_ctrl_clk_stop_cla_clock_stop_test`, `dtp_dbg_ctrl_clk_stop_random_clock_stop_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-DBG-TDR`, `CHK-DBG-PIN` (`stop_clks` polled, `cla_clock_stop_en`). cocotb: plain assertions |`c_jtag_clock_stop_*`, `c_cla_clock_stop_en_*`, `c_cla_readback_*`, `c_clk_stop_req_bit_0` to `c_clk_stop_req_bit_8`, `c_clk_stop_req_{none,multi_mask}`, `c_clock_stop_output_*` |YES; also the F5.7 credit
|F2.4 DEBUG_CONTROL boot stall: the four override and stall combinations and their independence from the clock-stop fields |`dtp_dbg_ctrl_boot_stall_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-DBG-TDR`, `CHK-DBG-PIN` (`jtag_boot_stall`, `jtag_boot_stall_ovrd`). cocotb: plain assertions |`c_boot_stall_ovrd{0,1}_stall{0,1}`, `c_boot_stall_{standalone,with_jtag_stop,with_cla_en,with_both}` |YES
|F2.5 Capability TDRs are read-only and stable |`dtp_dbg_jtag_caps_test`, `dtp_dbg_smc_jtag2axi_caps_test`, `dtp_dbg_smc_otp_jtag2axi_caps_test`, `dtp_dbg_sep_otp_jtag2axi_caps_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-CAPS` (the packed value and each field, the repeated reads, the reads after IDCODE and BYPASS), `CHK-CAPS-RO` (each write-attempt pattern). cocotb: plain assertions |`c_caps_read_*`, `c_caps_repeated_read`, `c_caps_read_after_{idcode,bypass}`, `c_caps_write_attempt_*` |YES
|===

One hook proves that the evidence path gates the verdict on SV-UVM:
`+DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` corrupts every family expectation
(TDR fields, pin observables, capability values), and a run with it set must
fail. The cocotb realization attaches no family checker in this section, so
the same environment variable does not reach it and a cocotb run with the
hook set passes; the cocotb twins are falsifiable only through their plain
assertions, which fail on any mismatch but cannot be armed.

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_jtag_ic_reset_test --plusarg +DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_dbg_jtag_caps_test --plusarg +DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE
----

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-dbg-lifecycle]]
===== Reset precedence and lifecycle review

The reset sources the section 1 and section 0 scenarios drive, what each
must do to the debug state, and where the behavior is proven:

[cols="1,3,2",options="header",]
|===
|Reset source |Required effect |Evidence
|Power-on reset alone (TRST held high) |Test-Logic-Reset, IDCODE selected |`dtp_jtag_trst_por_independence_test` (`CHK-TAP-POR-TLR`); T3 `ast_trst_or_por_resets_tap`
|TRST |Test-Logic-Reset, IDCODE selected; IC_RESET restored to its default including `reset_hold`; the PTAP and STAP 3DCRs cleared whatever `config_hold` holds |`dtp_jtag_trst_test`, `dtp_jtag_ic_reset_test` (`CHK-DBG-TDR` after TRST), `dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test`; T3 `ast_ic_reset_trst_clears_all`
|Test-Logic-Reset by five TMS-high clocks |IDCODE selected; the TMP state unchanged; IC_RESET unchanged with `reset_hold=0` and restored with `reset_hold=1`; the 3DCRs held with `config_hold=1` and cleared with `config_hold=0` |`dtp_jtag_tlr_reset_test`, `dtp_jtag_ic_reset_test` (`CHK-DBG-TDR` for both `reset_hold` values), `dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test`; T3 `ast_tmp_survives_tlr`, `ast_ic_reset_hold_blocks_tlr`, `ast_3dcr_config_hold_blocks_tlr`
|Chip reset (`chrst_n_in`) |The TMP persistence state survives and TAP access continues |`dtp_jtag_tmp_status_chrst_n_in_persistence_test` (`CHK-TMP-PERSIST` after the pulse); T3 `ast_chrst_follows_rst_unless_persistent`
|System reset on the AXI and cross-trigger side (`rst_n_i`) |Every cross-trigger CSR and CTP state returns to its reset value with no activity across the window; a JTAG2AXI bridge in flight clears through its CDC |`dtp_xtrig_reset_test`, the three `dtp_ctm_reset_*` scenarios (`CHK-XTRIG-QUIET`, `CHK-XTRIG-CSR`); the two reset-abort scenarios drive the JTAG2AXI mid-flight case (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-j2a-dispositions>>). Every scenario that drives `rst_n_i` or `pwr_on_rst_ni` also records the tb_top reset-assertion counter advancing across the pulse (`CHK-RESET-COUNT`), so a reset claim cannot be met by a reset that never asserted
|CTP `CONFIG.RESET` |A stalled point-to-point handshake is force-cleared and the port resumes |`dtp_xtrig_reset_test`
|===

The precedence follows the reset hierarchy the T3 properties state: TRST and
power-on reset are the root and each alone forces Test-Logic-Reset; the
Test-Logic-Reset state drives the TDR reset below it, which the TMP
persistence, the IC_RESET `reset_hold`, and the 3DCR `config_hold` each
exempt one register from; the chip reset sits below the TMP state and never
clears it while persistence is on.

Status and clear behavior. DEBUG_CONTROL bit 4 (`cla_clock_stop`) is a
read-only status captured on Capture-DR from the synchronized OR of the CLA
requests; the shifted value is ignored and the bit follows the requests up
and down (`dtp_dbg_ctrl_clk_stop_cla_clock_stop_test`,
`dtp_dbg_ctrl_clk_stop_random_clock_stop_test`). The TMP persistence bit is
the one sticky state in the section: it is set only by CLAMP_HOLD, cleared
only by CLAMP_RELEASE or by the armed BYPASS escape (the escape bit written
through TMP_STATUS and consumed by the BYPASS double load), and preserved
across Test-Logic-Reset and the chip reset. No debug TDR uses a
write-one-to-clear field; the sticky series status of the bridges, cleared
through `SERIES_CTRL.reset`, is the section 2 to 4 credit.

Lifecycle gates. The section 1 TDRs carry no `dbg_disable` field: TMP_STATUS,
IC_RESET, DEBUG_CONTROL, and the capability registers are reachable whenever
their instruction is enabled by its elaboration parameter, and a disabled
instruction selects BYPASS (`c_ir_behavior_disabled_to_bypass`, T2). The
eleven `dbg_disable_t` fields gate the scan and bridge resources, and every
gate has enabled, blocked, and restored evidence in the groups that own them:

[cols="1,1,3,2",options="header",]
|===
|Resource |Field |Enabled, blocked, and restored evidence |Closure
|Primary TAP and its TDRs |none |Always reachable; instruction enables are elaboration parameters |T2 `ast_undefined_selects_bypass`
|I/O, SMC, SEP, and extra STAP |`stap_io`, `stap_smc`, `stap_sep`, `stap_extra` |Forwarding and downstream readback while selected (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`, `CHK-DS-*`); no forwarding, a parked TMS, an ignored 3DCR update, and a downstream register frozen in Test-Logic-Reset while gated (`CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`, `CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`); the stored selection resumes without reset (`CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME`) |`dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_*`, `dtp_scan_dbg_disable_matrix_test`; eight of the sixteen scan-side ledger cells
|Extended STAP host scan path |`stap_host` |Scan controls pulse and data returns while enabled; all four controls quiet and the scan-in looped back while gated; resumption without reset (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`) |`dtp_ext_stap_scan_test`, `dtp_scan_dbg_disable_matrix_test`; two ledger cells
|DFT secure, DFT non-secure, and DFD instruments |`dft_secure`, `dft_nonsecure`, `dfd` |Instrument access with the SIB open and ungated (`CHK-SCAN-LEN`, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`); a gated SIB captures closed and its four scan controls never pulse (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`); a gated open attempt does not stick and access resumes without reset |`dtp_ijtag_dft_test`, `dtp_ijtag_dfd_test`, `dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test`, `dtp_scan_dbg_disable_matrix_test`; six ledger cells
|SMC fabric, SMC OTP, and SEP OTP bridges |`smc_jtag2axi`, `smc_otp_jtag2axi`, `sep_otp_jtag2axi` |A counted baseline access while enabled; no TDR update and no bus request while gated (`CHK-AXI-NOACT`, `CHK-AXI-BLOCKED`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-*`); no replay after release and a completed recovery access |The nine `*_security_gating` scenarios, `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_read_security_gating_no_axi_activity_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_dbg_disable_matrix_test`; the six bridge ledger cells
|===

The 22-cell ledger of the two matrix scenarios is the closure contract: a
cell is recorded only after its checker passes, each matrix refuses to finish
with a missing cell, and the `dbg_disable` group runs both matrices with
every directed gating scenario. Leaked activity under a gate is a failure of
the quiet window, the no-activity record, or the blocked-window record, never
a coverage bin.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-dbg-dispositions]]
===== Plan-to-realization dispositions

Each row is a section 1 checker whose realization differs from its plan text
or whose credit rests on one realization, with where the behavior is proven.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Realization |Disposition
|Every checker of the twelve cocotb scenarios |The cocotb sequences compare through `assert_equal`: a `CHECK <name> expected= observed=` log line and a Python assertion per comparison, no attached family checker, no `CHK-*` record, no `CHECKER_SUMMARY`; a corrupted expectation cannot be armed, so `DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` leaves a cocotb run passing. The SV-UVM twins record `CHK-DBG-TDR`, `CHK-DBG-PIN`, `CHK-CAPS`, `CHK-CAPS-RO`, `CHK-TMP-PERSIST`, and `CHK-TMP-ESCAPE` through the family checker and fail under the hook |Named evidence on the SV-UVM twin; the cocotb realization compares through plain assertions (TB_ARCH, realization differences)
|`tmp_status_side_effect_chk`, `tmp_persistence_side_effect_chk`, `bypass_escape_side_effect_chk`, `jtag_caps_side_effect_chk`, `smc_jtag2axi_caps_side_effect_chk`, `sep_otp_jtag2axi_caps_side_effect_chk`, `smc_otp_jtag2axi_caps_side_effect_chk` |The TMP scenarios read IDCODE after the TMP_STATUS accesses (`CHK-DBG-TDR` on the marker bit) and the escape scenario runs a BYPASS scan after the escape (`CHK-BYPASS-DELAY`); the capability scenarios re-read the register after IDCODE and BYPASS (`CHK-CAPS`). Scan routing has no observable beyond these reads |Credited through the re-reads
|`ic_reset_slice_chk` (the `IC_RESET_{SMC,SEP,EXT}_ENABLE` gating) |The standalone bench elaborates one enabled port per slice; each slice is proven to override independently (`CHK-DBG-PIN` per slice), and a slice with its enable parameter off has no realization on this elaboration |Credited for the independent override; the disabled-parameter path is outside this elaboration (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-quality>>, disabled elaboration parameters)
|`jtag_caps_fields_chk` |SV-UVM compares the packed value and every field of the `[59:0]` layout against the elaboration constants of the standalone DTP (`CHK-CAPS` per field); cocotb compares the same fields against its DV-owned expected table. Both tables transcribe the "JTAG Capabilities" table of the PTAP document |Credited
|`clk_stop_random_halt_chk`, `clk_stop_random_readback_chk` ("bit-accurate reference model") |The expected `stop_clks` is the OR of the programmed `jtag_clock_stop` bit and the driven request vector, and the expected `cla_clock_stop` readback is the OR of the requests, both computed by the sequence from its own stimulus and compared after the synchronizer poll |Credited; the prediction is the sequence's, not a model class
|===

===== Coverage review

The `debug_tdr` group owns every point of
`dtp_debug_tdr_fcov`: the TMP persistence, read-value, and escape points,
the IC_RESET slice, override, assertion, and hold points, the four
boot-stall combinations and their four interaction points, the clock-stop
enable, readback, and output points with each of the nine request bits and
the none and multi-mask points, and the capability read, re-read,
instruction-switch, and write-attempt points. In `dtp_fcov` it owns the
`c_ir_opcode_*` points of TMP_STATUS, IC_RESET, DEBUG_CONTROL, JTAG_CAPS,
and the three JTAG2AXI capability TDRs, and `c_ir_category_debug` and
`c_ir_category_jtag2axi`. On the commercial simulators the
`cg_tmp_ic_reset`, `cg_debug_control`, and `cg_caps_tdr` covergroups carry
the same bins and add the parameter-derived JTAG_CAPS field bins, the bus
type and geometry bins, and the per-register capture events.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-j2a]]
==== JTAG2AXI and OTP bridges (sections 2 to 4)

The section 2, 3, and 4 scenarios are the `jtag2axi` group and realize the
F4 rows of the scope matrix: single-operation and series transfers on the
SMC fabric AXI4 bridge and the SMC and SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridges, the
SUCCESS, SLVERR, DECERR, and BUSY_OR_FULL status classes, backpressure and
reset recovery, and the per-bridge lifecycle gate. `functional` carries the
SMC fabric single write and single write-read as bring-up. The formal plan's
target T7 (<<dtp-formal-targets>>) proves the bridge control machine; these
rows record the simulation evidence beside it.

Every pass opens with the geometry gate of the JTAG2AXI base sequence: the
three `*_JTAG2AXI_CAPS` TDRs are read and `CHK-J2A-GEOMETRY` compares the
bus type, address size, and data size of each bridge with the DV geometry
table before any request is packed. Every scenario opts into the shared
`ocah_axi_vip` passive environment on each bridge port it drives (monitor,
reference model, scoreboard, checker), declares the records it requires, and
sets a minimum number of compared transactions per armed stream, so a silent
run fails at finalization. The sequence adds `CHK-AXI-NONVAC` (every armed
