error was consumed), `CHK-AXI-CREDITS` (the injection budget was spent),
`CHK-AXI-NOACT` (a gated bridge raised no request), the scoreboard's blocked
window (`CHK-AXI-BLOCKED`, which fails on any monitored transaction across a
gated attempt and its release), and `CHK-AXI-WMEM` (the responder memory
holds the written word), and records its own judgements into the same
finalization: `CHK-J2A-FAULT-STATUS` (the op-status or SERIES_CTRL status
carries the injected error code), `CHK-J2A-STATUS-BIT` (the WITH_ERROR_STATUS
bit on every shift of a with-error series), `CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA` (the errored
read beat's data), `CHK-J2A-SERIES-ADDR` (the SERIES_CTRL address after every
beat), and the `CHK-J2A-ABORT-*` and `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR` records of the
reset-abort scenarios. On SV-UVM the always-on `dtp_scoreboard` features
`jtag2axi_req`, `jtag2axi_status`, and `ir_decode` end every run as
`CHK-SB-JTAG2AXI-REQ`, `CHK-SB-JTAG2AXI-STATUS`, and `CHK-SB-IR-DECODE`;
`CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ` records each injected error, and the `CHK-AXI-GATE-*`
records prove exact request-count deltas across a gate. Each procedure's
*Evidence* field names the records it lands.

[cols="1,2,3,2,1",options="header",]
|===
|Row |Tests |Named evidence |Coverage points |Credit
|F4.1 Single-operation write and read with data verification |The `single_write`, `single_write_data_verify`, and `single_write_read` scenarios of the three targets |`CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STRB`, `CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RDATA`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN`; SV-UVM `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-RADDR` and the `jtag2axi_req` and `jtag2axi_status` features |`+c_<target>_op_{write,read,nop}+`, `+c_<target>_wstrb_*+`, `+c_<target>_addr_*+`, the size points, `+c_<target>_status_success+`, `+c_<target>_poll_{immediate,short_wait}+` |YES
|F4.2 Series operations |The `series_write_{incr,no_incr,incr_with_error}`, `series_write_read_{incr,no_incr,incr_with_error}`, `random_ops`, and `read_random_ops` scenarios of the three targets, the two SMC fabric `narrow` scenarios, `dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test` |As F4.1; `CHK-J2A-STATUS-BIT` on the six `with_error` scenarios, each with one SLVERR or DECERR beat armed in its stream |`+c_<target>_series_{incr,no_incr,with_error_status}+` |YES for the three series modes; the series-corner procedure credits the address progression and the terminal status per bridge
|F4.3 Response status: SLVERR and DECERR, the error-status TDR, recovery |The `error_*` scenarios of the three targets, the six `with_error` series scenarios, and the three `decode_error_*` scenarios |The armed non-OKAY response (`CHK-AXI-RESP-EXPECTED`, SV-UVM `CHK-AXI-ERR-INJ`), `CHK-AXI-CREDITS`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-J2A-FAULT-STATUS`, `CHK-J2A-STATUS-BIT`, `CHK-J2A-ERR-RDATA`, `CHK-J2A-SERIES-ADDR`; the `jtag2axi_status` feature |`+c_<target>_status_{slverr,decerr}+`, `+c_<target>_recovery_next_rw_after_error+` |YES
|F4.4 Backpressure and reset recovery |`dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_aw_before_w_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_long_stall_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_abort_at_data_w_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_narrow_reset_mid_xaction_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test` |`CHK-AXI-COMPLETION`, `CHK-AXI-RESP`, `CHK-AXI-STREAM-MIN` on all three streams; the protocol SVA handshake-hold rules; `CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`, `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` on the two reset-abort scenarios; SV-UVM `CHK-AXI-WADDR`, `CHK-AXI-WDATA`, `CHK-AXI-WMEM` |`+c_<target>_skew_*+`, `+c_<target>_status_busy_or_full+`, `+c_<target>_reset_timing_idle+`, `+c_<target>_recovery_reset_abort+`, `c_reset_back_to_back`; the request-issued, data-phase, response-wait, CDC clear, and CDC isolate points are sampled only by the two reset-abort scenarios; the request-issued, response-wait, and CDC clear points carry dispositions in `coverage_policy.toml` |YES for skew and a reset after completion; NO for a reset that lands mid-transaction, which the two reset-abort scenarios drive (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-j2a-dispositions>>)
|F4.5 Per-bridge lifecycle gate |The `write_security_gating`, `read_security_gating`, and `error_security_gating` scenarios of the three targets, `dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_read_security_gating_no_axi_activity_test`, `dtp_jtag2axi_dbg_disable_matrix_test` |`CHK-AXI-NOACT`, `CHK-AXI-BLOCKED`, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC`; SV-UVM `CHK-AXI-GATE-AW`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-W`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-AR`, `CHK-AXI-GATE-EXACT`; the ledger's six bridge cells |`+c_<target>_gated_attempt+`, `+c_<target>_gating_release_no_replay+`, `+c_<target>_gating_recovery+` |YES
|===

The bridge geometry the checks encode (bus type, address width, and data
width per bridge) lives in one DV-owned table per realization and is
cross-checked against each bridge's capability TDR by the geometry gate
before every pass and by the section 1 capability scenarios. The responder
memory and the design collateral are the other sources of expected values;
none is derived from the observation under check.

Four hooks prove that the evidence path gates the verdict; a run with any of
them set must fail:

* `DTP_AXI_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` (an environment variable on cocotb, a
  plusarg on SV-UVM) arms the wrong expected response for an injected
  error, so the passive environment's response compare fails.
* `DTP_J2A_REF_MODEL_NEGATIVE` (SV-UVM) makes the bridge request reference
  model predict corrupted addresses, so the `jtag2axi_req` pairing fails.
* `DTP_J2A_GEOMETRY_NEGATIVE` (both realizations) corrupts the expected
  address size of the geometry gate, so `CHK-J2A-GEOMETRY` fails before any
  bridge request is packed.
* `DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE` (both realizations) leaves the with-error
  series fault unarmed while its expectation stands, so `CHK-J2A-STATUS-BIT`
  fails on the shift after the fault beat.

The blocked-activity path has no separate hook: each gating scenario
carries its positive control, an enabled baseline access with counted
request activity before the disable, so a request counter that does not
move under the disable is judged against one that did.

[source,bash]
----
DTP_AXI_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test
DTP_AXI_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_single_write_test
DTP_J2A_GEOMETRY_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_single_write_read_test
DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_series_write_incr_with_error_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_error_single_write_test --plusarg +DTP_AXI_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_axi_single_write_read_test --plusarg +DTP_J2A_REF_MODEL_NEGATIVE
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_single_write_read_test --plusarg +DTP_J2A_GEOMETRY_NEGATIVE
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_jtag2axi_smc_otp_axi_series_write_incr_with_error_test --plusarg +DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE
----

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-j2a-dispositions]]
===== Plan-to-realization dispositions

Each row is a checker of sections 2 to 4 whose realization differs from
its plan text or whose credit is withheld, with where the behavior is
proven and what is not credited.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Realization |Disposition
|`j2a_bp_stable_chk` (4.28), `j2a_long_stall_assert_chk` (4.29) |The protocol SVA handshake-hold and payload-stability rules on the bridge ports; `dtp_tb_if` exposes each bridge's FSM state, pending flag, and CDC clear-seen flag, which the two reset-abort scenarios sample |Credited through the protocol SVA
|`CHK-J2A-ABORT-MIDFLIGHT`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-FSM`, `CHK-J2A-CDC-CLEAR`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-ESCAPE`, `CHK-J2A-ABORT-RECOVERY` (4.30, 4.31) |The READY stall is released only after the system reset, so the reset lands with the FSM observed outside `AXI_IDLE`; after the reset the FSM holds `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` with status `BUSY_OR_FULL` until a TAP reset, no write escapes, and the CDC clear is observed |Credited for the mid-flight observation, the escape containment, and the CDC clear; the FSM return to `AXI_IDLE` and the status recovery after a mid-transaction reset are not credited, and the request-issued, response-wait, and CDC clear points carry dispositions in `coverage_policy.toml`
|`j2a_long_stall_status_chk`, `j2a_long_stall_data_chk` |The stall outlasts the scan tail by the configured number of scans and the long-wait poll class fires on every bridge |Credited
|`*_wr_caps_chk` (2.1, 2.7, 2.13) |The write procedures load no capability TDR of their own; the geometry gate reads the three CAPS TDRs before every pass (`CHK-J2A-GEOMETRY`), and `dtp_dbg_smc_jtag2axi_caps_test`, `dtp_dbg_smc_otp_jtag2axi_caps_test`, and `dtp_dbg_sep_otp_jtag2axi_caps_test` read them as TDRs |Credited through the gate and under F2.5
|===

===== Coverage review

The `jtag2axi` group owns, for each of the three bridges,
every operation, status, poll, series, skew, recovery, gated-attempt,
gating, write-strobe, address-class, and size point of
`dtp_jtag2axi_fcov`, and the `c_reset_back_to_back` point; the
`poll_long_wait` point of each bridge belongs to the long-stall scenario.
The request-issued, data-phase, and response-wait reset-timing points and
the CDC clear and isolate points of each bridge are sampled only by the two
reset-abort scenarios; the request-issued, response-wait, and CDC clear
points carry dispositions in `cov/config/verilator/coverage_policy.toml`. On the
commercial simulators the module's `jtag2axi` covergroups carry the same
bins.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-scan]]
==== STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG scan routing (section 5)

The section 5 scenarios are the `scan` group and realize the F3 rows of the
scope matrix: the IEEE 1687 SIB network and its three instruments, the
per-instrument disables, STAP selection through the composed TAP_3DCR chain
for the I/O, SMC, SEP, and extra ports, the extended host scan path, the
3DCR `config_hold` and `tms_hold` fields, and the eight scan-side lifecycle
gates. The `dbg_disable` group carries the scan matrix and the directed
gating scenarios into the closure contract. The formal targets T4 (STAP
selection and debug disable) and T5 (the SIB network) corroborate F3.1 to
F3.6 where SymbiYosys is installed and are not regression credit.

Topology and model assumptions. The bench's iJTAG network is the three SIBs
of the interface unit in chain order DFT secure, DFT non-secure, DFD, each
with a loopback instrument stub of 4, 5, and 6 bits in `tb_top`, so every
subset of open SIBs has a distinct chain length; the observe scan is 40 bits
with a 16-bit marker word ahead of the chain image, and the SIB model
predicts the chain image, the capture of each SIB, and the value an open
stub latched on the previous scan. The STAP network is the 2-bit PTAP 3DCR
(select, `config_hold`) followed serially by the STAP SIB chain of the four
ports in the order I/O, SMC, SEP, extra, each STAP carrying a 3-bit 3DCR
(`stap_sel`, `tms_hold`, `config_hold`), the selected I/O STAP splicing one
lockup flop, and every scan issued while a STAP is selected composed over
the full network, IR scans included. Behind each host port the four
STAP-selection scenarios attach a behavioral `ocah_jtag_vip` slave device
with a 5-bit IR, a distinct IDCODE, and one writable `DS_TDR` of a distinct
width per port; every other scenario sees the host ports as loopbacks. Both
models are DUT-local (TB_ARCH, DUT-local model ownership): they transcribe
the scan-chain topology and the iJTAG network of the `jtag_intf_unit`
chapter and the 3DCR register and lockup latches of the `jtag_stap` chapter,
and they predict from the sequence's programming intent, never from the
observed scan. The downstream device table (IDCODE and `DS_TDR` width per
port) is one parity contract between `cocotb/env/dtp_stap_ds_agent.py` and
`uvm/env/dtp_env.svh`.

Every scenario runs at least sixteen seeded passes. Both realizations attach
the family checker with the scenario's required IDs (without the scan
cross-check, because the composed raw-TMS scans are not countable by the
sequence) and record every comparison through it: `CHK-SCAN-WIN` counts the
pulses of the host scan controls, `tdo_oen`, and `tms` inside a temporal
window and judges them quiet or active, `CHK-SCAN-LEN` is the marker latency
against the predicted chain length, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` is a captured chain
image against the model, `CHK-SCAN-OBS` is the PTAP 3DCR read over the TDR
return path, and the `CHK-DS-*` and `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE*` records are the
downstream device's IDCODE, `DS_TDR` readbacks, and its own Update-DR
record. A pass with zero checks or a missing required ID fails at
finalization. On SV-UVM the env checker adds `CHK-SCAN-IR-LEN` and
`CHK-SCAN-DR-LEN` for every reconstructed scan, the always-on
`dtp_scoreboard` ends the run with `CHK-SB-IR-DECODE`, and the env TAP
checker with `CHK-TAP-STATE`. Each procedure's *Evidence* field names the
records it lands.

[cols="1,2,3,2,1",options="header",]
|===
|Row |Tests |Named evidence |Coverage points |Credit
|F3.1 iJTAG SIB network: every SIB pattern, chain length, instrument capture |`dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test`, `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test`, `dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test` |`CHK-SCAN-LEN`, `CHK-SCAN-WIN`, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`, `CHK-TAP-RESET-TLR` |`c_sib_pattern_dfd{0,1}_dfts{0,1}_dftn{0,1}` (all eight), `c_instrument_access_{dfd,dft_secure,dft_nonsecure}` |YES against the 4-, 5-, and 6-bit loopback stubs (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-scan-dispositions>>)
|F3.2 Instrument access follows `dft_secure`, `dft_nonsecure`, and `dfd` |`dtp_ijtag_dft_test`, `dtp_ijtag_dfd_test`; the random and all-on scenarios under seeded masks |`CHK-SCAN-WIN` (a gated SIB's four controls quiet, the other SIB still openable), `CHK-SCAN-LEN`, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` (a gated open attempt not stuck after release) |`c_sib_gating_{enabled,gated}_{dfd,dft_secure,dft_nonsecure}`, `c_dbg_disable_{dfd,dft_secure,dft_nonsecure}_{clear,set}` |YES
|F3.3 STAP selection for the four ports: forwarding while selected, parked while gated, recovery without reset |`dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test`, `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_smc_test`, `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_sep_test`, `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_extra_test` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN`, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`, `CHK-DS-IDCODE`, `CHK-DS-TDR-READBACK`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE`, `CHK-SLAVE-DR-UPDATE-COUNT`, `CHK-DS-TDR-HOLD`, `CHK-DS-PARKED-TLR`, `CHK-DS-TDR-RESUME` |`c_stap_type_{io,smc,sep,extra}`, `c_3dcr_field_select`, `c_3dcr_chain_{sib_open,sib_closed,selected_stap_in_path}`, `c_stap_security_{enabled,gated_by_field}`, `c_dbg_disable_stap_{io,smc,sep,extra}_{clear,set}` |YES against the behavioral downstream TAPs
|F3.4 The extended host scan path follows `stap_host` |`dtp_ext_stap_scan_test` |`CHK-SCAN-WIN` (the host `select` and `shift_en` active, all four controls quiet while gated, active again after release) |`c_dbg_disable_stap_host_{clear,set}` |YES for the control path; the data path is the loopback (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-scan-dispositions>>)
|F3.5 3DCR `config_hold` and `tms_hold` |`dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test`, `dtp_3dcr_tms_hold_test` |`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`, `CHK-SCAN-OBS` (the TDR return path after a cleared select), `CHK-SCAN-WIN` (the parked TMS polarity) |`c_3dcr_field_{tms_hold,config_hold}` |YES
|F3.6 Every scan-side disable gates exactly its path, with no replay after release and full recovery |`dtp_scan_dbg_disable_matrix_test` |SV-UVM: `CHK-SCAN-WIN`, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`, `CHK-SCAN-LEN` per row. cocotb: the sixteen scan-side ledger cells (`require_cells`) and the JSON artifact; the per-row comparisons are plain assertions |The eight scan-side `c_dbg_disable_<field>_{clear,set}` mirrors |YES; the closure contract is the cocotb ledger, the named evidence is the SV-UVM twin's (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-scan-dispositions>>)
|===

Every instrument and every STAP port has positive and blocked evidence in
the same scenario: the SIB scenarios open the instrument and record its
select before gating it and recording the quiet window, and the
STAP-selection scenarios read the downstream TAP through the port before
gating it and recording the parked TMS, the frozen downstream register, and
the ignored 3DCR update. The matrix repeats both outcomes per field under
the one-hot, boundary, and seeded multi-hot masks.

One hook proves that the evidence path gates the verdict in both
realizations: `DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` (an environment variable
on cocotb, a plusarg on SV-UVM) corrupts every family expectation, so the
window counts, the marker latency, the chain images, and the downstream
readbacks all fail, and a run with it set must fail. The cocotb scan matrix
attaches no family checker, so the hook does not reach it; its fail-closed
path is the ledger's refusal to finish with a missing cell and the plain
assertions of each row.

[source,bash]
----
DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test
DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test --plusarg +DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test --plusarg +DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE
----

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-scan-dispositions]]
===== Plan-to-realization dispositions

Each row is a section 5 checker whose realization differs from its plan
text or whose credit is scoped, with where the behavior is proven and the
scope of any exclusion.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Realization |Disposition
|The checkers of 5.2, 5.4, 5.5, 5.10, and 5.13 named without an evidence ID |Every one is realized under `CHK-SCAN-WIN`, `CHK-SCAN-LEN`, or `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN` through the shared scan helpers: chain length as the marker latency, scan control as a counted window, gating as a quiet window plus a chain capture, recovery as the active window after release |Credited under the three scan records
|`ext_stap_enable_chk` (scan data through the host scan-out and scan-in) |The host scan-in is the loopback of the host scan-out in `tb_top`, so no independent data observable exists; the scenario records the host `select` and `shift_en` window (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`) and samples the `jtag_stap_host_select` level after each shift as a plain assertion |Credited for the control path; the data path has no observable on this bench (scope exclusion below)
|`sib_all_on_gate_chk`, `sib_all_on_restore_chk`, `dft_*_gate_chk`, `dft_gate_restore_chk`, `dfd_gate_chk`, `dfd_stored_chk`, `dfd_restore_chk` |A gated SIB is proven closed by its quiet window (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`), the collapsed chain length (`CHK-SCAN-LEN`), and its captured state (`CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`); release without reset is proven by the active window and the full length again, and the stored-state rows by a capture that shows the gated open attempt did not latch |Credited
|`stap_*_isolation_chk` |The scenario configures the neighbor STAP while the target stays gated and reads the neighbor's downstream TAP through a full load, write, and read cycle (`CHK-DS-*` on the neighbor) with the target's port quiet (`CHK-SCAN-WIN`) and its device recording no update |Credited
|`scan_matrix_*_chk` (cocotb) |The cocotb matrix compares through plain assertions (the shared window and chain helpers fall back to `assert_equal` without an attached checker) and closes through the ledger's sixteen required cells and the JSON artifact; the SV-UVM matrix records `CHK-SCAN-WIN`, `CHK-SCAN-CHAIN`, and `CHK-SCAN-LEN` per row and has no ledger |Credited: the closure contract is the cocotb ledger, the named evidence is the SV-UVM twin's; the two realizations carry complementary halves of the F3.6 proof
|Instrument and downstream TAP behavior beyond the stubs |The instruments behind the SIBs are 4-, 5-, and 6-bit loopback stubs, the downstream TAPs are behavioral VIP devices with one writable register, and the extended host scan-in is a loopback; the real SMC and SEP debug TAPs and any instrument are integration-level |Out of bench scope: the scan scenarios prove selection, routing, gating, chain length, and the downstream protocol against the bench's stubs and devices; instrument data and the SMC and SEP debug TAPs are verified at integration
|===

===== Coverage review

The `scan` group owns every point of `dtp_scan_fcov`
except the three bridge-field mirrors (`c_dbg_disable_smc_jtag2axi_set`,
`c_dbg_disable_smc_otp_jtag2axi_set`, `c_dbg_disable_sep_otp_jtag2axi_set`),
which the JTAG2AXI matrix owns in the `jtag2axi` and `dbg_disable` groups:
all eight SIB patterns, the three instrument-access points, the six SIB
gating points, the four STAP types, the three 3DCR field points, the three
chain points, the two STAP security points, and the sixteen scan-side field
mirrors. In `dtp_fcov` it owns the `c_ir_opcode_*` points of TAP_3DCR and
SELECT_IJTAG and `c_ir_category_ieee1838` and `c_ir_category_ijtag`. On the
commercial simulators the `cg_ijtag_sib` and `cg_stap_3dcr` covergroups
carry the same bins.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-xtrig]]
==== XTRIG, CTP, and CTM (section 6)

The section 6 scenarios are the `xtrig` group and realize the F5.1 to F5.6
rows of the scope matrix: CTP wire-OR pulses with the configurable
stretcher and inversion, the point-to-point four-phase handshake, CTM
routing over every source and destination index with fanout and isolation,
the CSR map with its byte strobes and reserved bits, the AXI-Lite CSR port
under channel skew, demux locking, and decode backpressure, and reset
recovery in each mode. F5.7, the clock-stop aggregation, is the section 1
credit. The formal targets T8 (`ctn_xtrig_fpv`) and T9 (`ctn_csr_fpv`)
corroborate these rows where SymbiYosys is installed and are not regression
credit.

Topology and register map. The bench elaborates the cross-trigger network
with sixteen external CTPs on matrix ports 0 to 15 and ten internal
cross-trigger channels on ports 16 to 25. `+CT_SRC[k]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+`
is the register of matrix output `k` and holds one bit per input port whose
pulses are OR'd onto that output; each CTP carries CONFIG (`MODE`, `INVERT`,
`RESET`), the five read-only STATUS bits, and STRETCH_MULT. The CSR port is
the shared `ocah_axi_vip` AXI-Lite master into the crossbar, whose error
subordinate answers every unmapped word with DECERR and its fixed data word.
The route helpers program every external CTP they use with `STRETCH_MULT=1`,
so a routed pulse is two cycles wide on every port. The CTP and CTM "BFMs"
of the plan are the bench's pin drivers and monitors on `dtp_xtrig_if`; the
cross-trigger port, matrix, and network under test are the RTL, and no stub
stands in for a design block.

Every scenario runs at least sixteen seeded passes. Both realizations own a
named-evidence checker with the scenario's required IDs and finalize it once
per pass, so a pass with zero checks or a missing required ID fails.
`CHK-XTRIG-CSR` is a CSR readback against the value written, masked by the
field masks of the SystemRDL; `CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL` is a pad or handshake level
at the port's polarity; `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL` is the set of outputs that
fired inside an activity window against the prediction of the CTM model,
which is programmed from the selects the sequence writes and never from an
observed pulse; `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION` is every unselected output quiet in
the same window; `CHK-XTRIG-QUIET` is every request and acknowledge output
and every busy flop quiet across a reset window or an access;
`CHK-XTRIG-STRETCH` is a measured enable width; `CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`,
`CHK-XTRIG-AW-LOCK`, and `CHK-XTRIG-AR-STALL` are the AXI-Lite responses,
the demux state mirrors, and the stall counters. The activity window ORs
the matrix request outputs and the CTP request pads per cycle from before
the input pulse until the drain tail, so the observation is the pins through
`dtp_xtrig_if` and not the CSR readback. On SV-UVM the always-on
`dtp_scoreboard` adds an independent shadow predictor on the passive XTRIG
monitor stream and ends every run with the `xtrig_csr` feature as
`CHK-SB-XTRIG-CSR`, and the read-decode scenario with the `xtrig_decode`
feature as `CHK-SB-XTRIG-DECODE`; the cocotb realization has no scoreboard
feature for the CSR port and its CSR credit is the checker's record. Each
procedure's *Evidence* field names the records it lands.

[cols="1,2,3,2,1",options="header",]
|===
|Row |Tests |Named evidence |Coverage points |Credit
|F5.1 CTP wire-OR: pulse routing, the stretcher, inversion |`dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test`, the four `dtp_ctm_wire_or_*` scenarios, `dtp_ctm_rand_wire_or_only_test`, the wire-OR draws of `dtp_xtrig_rand_test` |`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`, `CHK-XTRIG-STRETCH`, `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION`, `CHK-XTRIG-CSR` (STATUS.BUSY during a wide pulse) |`c_ctp_mode_wire_or`, `c_ctp_inversion_{normal,inverted}`, `c_ctp_stretch_{min,mid,max}` |YES
|F5.2 CTP point-to-point four-phase handshake |`dtp_xtrig_p2p_test`, the four `dtp_ctm_p2p_*` scenarios, `dtp_ctm_rand_p2p_only_test`, the point-to-point draws of `dtp_xtrig_rand_test` |`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL` (both handshake directions at the pads), `CHK-XTRIG-CSR` (the five STATUS bits per phase), `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL` |`c_ctp_mode_p2p`, `c_ctp_p2p_state_{idle,request_phase,acknowledge_phase,reset_recovery}` |YES
|F5.3 CTM routing: every source and destination index, fanout, isolation |`dtp_xtrig_rand_test`, `dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_dst_port_sweep_test`, `dtp_ctm_rand_all_scenarios_test`, `dtp_ctm_rand_cla_to_ctp_test`, `dtp_ctm_rand_ctp_to_cla_test`, `dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test` |`CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`, `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION`, `CHK-XTRIG-CSR` |`c_ctm_source_type_{ctp,internal}`, `c_ctm_dest_type_{ctp,internal}`, `c_ctm_fanout_{none,single,multicast}`, `c_ctm_source_index_*` and `c_ctm_dest_index_*` for all twenty-six ports |YES
|F5.4 CSR map: readback, byte enables, reserved bits, no aliasing, reset defaults |`dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test`, `dtp_ctm_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test`, `dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test`, `dtp_ctm_rand_all_source_select_coverage_test` |`CHK-XTRIG-CSR`, `CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`; SV-UVM `CHK-SB-XTRIG-CSR` |The index points above; no dedicated CSR point |YES
|F5.5 AXI-Lite CSR port: channel skew, demux lock release, read-decode backpressure, DECERR for unmapped words |`dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test`, `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_demux_aw_lock_release_test`, `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_read_decode_backpressure_test` |`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`, `CHK-XTRIG-AW-LOCK`, `CHK-XTRIG-AR-STALL`; SV-UVM `CHK-SB-XTRIG-DECODE`; the `ocah_axi_sva` rules on the CSR port |None in `dtp_xtrig_fcov`; the protocol SVA carries the port |YES; the demux AW-lock branch is unreachable and not credited (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-xtrig-dispositions>>)
|F5.6 CTP and CTM reset recovery in each mode |`dtp_xtrig_reset_test`, `dtp_ctm_reset_wire_or_mode_test`, `dtp_ctm_reset_p2p_mode_test`, `dtp_ctm_reset_all_modes_test` |`CHK-XTRIG-QUIET` (the reset window), `CHK-XTRIG-CSR` (every register at its reset value), `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL` (the post-reset route) |`c_ctp_p2p_state_reset_recovery` |YES
|===

The deterministic and randomized coverage of sources and destinations is
complementary: the destination-port sweep walks every output from a seeded
internal source, the all-source-select scenario touches every source-select
register with three mask shapes, and the six random scenarios draw sources,
outputs, and modes per iteration, so every one of the twenty-six source and
twenty-six destination indices fires under both a directed and a seeded
stimulus.

Two hooks prove that the evidence path gates the verdict; each is an
environment variable on cocotb and a plusarg of the same name on SV-UVM,
and a run with either set must fail:

* `DTP_XTRIG_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` programs the CTM model with the inverted
  select of every route, so `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL` fails in every scenario
  that compares a route.
* `+DTP_XTRIG_NEGATIVE_CHECK=<n>+` corrupts the observed value of one
  evidence ID everywhere the scenario records it: 1 `CHK-XTRIG-CSR`, 2
  `CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`, 3 `CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`, 4 `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION`,
  5 `CHK-XTRIG-QUIET`, 6 `CHK-XTRIG-STRETCH`, 7 `CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`, 8
  `CHK-XTRIG-AW-LOCK`, 9 `CHK-XTRIG-AR-STALL`, so every record family,
  including the quiet windows and the AXI-Lite records the model hook does
  not touch, has its own must-fail proof.

[source,bash]
----
DTP_XTRIG_CHECKER_NEGATIVE=1 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_test
DTP_XTRIG_NEGATIVE_CHECK=7 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test
DTP_XTRIG_NEGATIVE_CHECK=6 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test
DTP_XTRIG_NEGATIVE_CHECK=5 python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --items dtp_ctm_reset_all_modes_test
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_ctm_p2p_cla_to_ctp_test --plusarg +DTP_XTRIG_CHECKER_NEGATIVE
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut dtp --framework uvm --items dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test --plusarg +DTP_XTRIG_NEGATIVE_CHECK=7
----

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-xtrig-dispositions]]
===== Plan-to-realization dispositions

Each row is a section 6 checker whose realization differs from its plan
text or whose credit is withheld, with where the behavior is proven.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Realization |Disposition
|`xtrig_reg_stall_unreachable_chk` (6.7) |The scenario drives seeded accepted-path writes and reads, records the quiet cross-trigger window across them (`CHK-XTRIG-QUIET`) and the advanced AWVALID and ARVALID counters (`CHK-XTRIG-AXIL`); no observable of the register block's local stall exists, and a simulation cannot prove a path unreachable |Credited for the accepted path; the unreachability of the local stall is a structural statement about the upstream ready and is not simulation credit
|`xtrig_demux_aw_lock_engage_chk` (6.9) |The engaged state the pair proves is the pending-W bookkeeping (`xtrig_demux_w_pending`) with the second AW held; the AW lock flag itself stays clear because every subordinate behind the crossbar accepts an AW into an empty slot in the same cycle, so the lock branch has no reachable stimulus from the DTP boundary |Credited for the pending-W engage and release and the ordering; the lock branch is not credited
|`ctp_wire_or_sync_chk`, `ctp_p2p_deliver_chk` (delivery to the CTM destination) |The delivered pulse is the output set of the activity window compared with the CTM model (`CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`) and the synchronized level at the pad (`CHK-XTRIG-SIGNAL`) |Credited
|The CSR readback checks of every scenario on cocotb |`CHK-XTRIG-CSR` compares the readback with the value the sequence wrote; the cocotb `DtpScoreboard` has no feature for the CSR port, while the SV-UVM `xtrig_csr` and `xtrig_decode` features pair the passive monitor stream with a shadow predictor |Credited in both; the independent predictor exists on the SV-UVM twin only, which the TB_ARCH realization differences record
|The CTP and CTM "BFMs" named in the procedures |Pin drivers and monitors on `dtp_xtrig_if` (`DtpXtrigAgent`, the SV-UVM pin surface of the base sequence) and the shared AXI-Lite master; no design block is stubbed |No stub receives credit; every record observes the RTL
|===

===== Coverage review

The `xtrig` group owns every point of `dtp_xtrig_fcov`:
both CTP modes, both inversions, the three stretch classes, the four
point-to-point states including reset recovery, both source and destination
types, the three fanout classes, and all twenty-six source and twenty-six
destination index points. F5.5 has no point in the module; the CSR port is
carried by the `ocah_axi_sva` rules and the `CHK-XTRIG-AXIL` records. On
the commercial simulators the `cg_ctp` and `cg_ctm` covergroups carry the
same bins.

[[dtp-vplan-signoff-quality]]
==== Test quality review and scope exclusions (all sections)

Every canonical scenario of `all` is reviewed for reproducible stimulus,
independent and non-trivial checking, diagnostic logs, legal interface use,
observability of the design rather than of the bench, and a proof that its
checking can fail. The scenarios of one family share a base sequence, an
evidence path, and a negative hook, so the review is recorded per family;
the per-scenario differences are the *Evidence* fields of the procedures and
the disposition tables of the component subsections.

[cols="2,2,3,2,2,2",options="header",]
|===
|Family (scenarios) |Stimulus and reproduction |Expected values and checker |Design observability |Interface legality |Must-fail proof
|Smoke and basic JTAG with the family checker (the section 0 scenarios with the family checker) |Sixteen passes per run, `scenario_seed = seed {plus} pass`, `DTP_RANDOM_COUNT` seeded patterns per pass, TCK and system-clock periods drawn by the test configuration; one seed reproduces one leaf |Opcodes, TAP-state encodings, and IDCODE fields transcribed from the PTAP and interface-unit documents; the VIP TAP model; the family checker with required IDs and the passive scan monitor cross-check |`jtag_ptap_state_o`, the decoded instruction, the BSR loopback, and the TMP state through `dtp_tb_if` |The VIP master driver; `ocah_jtag_sva` on the primary TAP |`DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE`, `DTP_JTAG_TAP_CHECKER_NEGATIVE`
|`dtp_sanity_test`, `dtp_jtag_undef_instr_test` |As above |cocotb: plain assertions of the sequence; SV-UVM: the TAP checker and the `bypass` and `idcode` features |As above |As above |SV-UVM only (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions>>)
|Debug TDRs (the section 1 scenarios) |As above; seeded IC_RESET patterns, clock-stop combinations, CAPS write-attempt patterns |TDR layouts and pin defaults from the PTAP document, the elaboration constants, the DV geometry table; cocotb: plain assertions; SV-UVM: the family checker |The TDR readbacks and the flattened pins through `dtp_tb_if`, polled across the synchronizer |The VIP master driver; `ocah_jtag_sva` |SV-UVM only (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-dbg-dispositions>>)
|JTAG2AXI bridges (the scenarios of sections 2 to 4) |As above; seeded addresses, data, strobes, operation mixes, error beats, skew and stall lengths |The responder memory backdoor, the DV geometry table, the armed error responses; the shared `ocah_axi_vip` passive environment per bridge port with required IDs and stream minimums; the sequence's own fault-beat judgements; SV-UVM: the bridge request and status features |The three bridge ports' monitors, the TDR status, and the FSM, pending, and CDC flags through `dtp_tb_if` |The VIP slave responders; `ocah_axi_sva` per bus |`DTP_AXI_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE`, `DTP_J2A_GEOMETRY_NEGATIVE`, `DTP_J2A_STATUS_BIT_NEGATIVE`; SV-UVM `DTP_J2A_REF_MODEL_NEGATIVE`
|Scan routing (the section 5 scenarios) |As above; seeded SIB patterns, disable masks, 3DCR payloads, marker words, downstream register values |The SIB and STAP models programmed from the sequence's intent; the downstream device table; the family checker |The host scan controls, `tms` and `tdo_oen` per port, the chain images, and the downstream devices' records |The VIP master driver and VIP slave devices; `ocah_jtag_sva` |`DTP_JTAG_FAMILY_CHECKER_NEGATIVE` in both realizations; the cocotb matrix through its ledger only (<<dtp-vplan-signoff-scan-dispositions>>)
|Cross-trigger (the section 6 scenarios) |As above; seeded ports, modes, stretch values, select masks, skew and hold lengths |The CTM model programmed from the written selects, the written CSR values under the SystemRDL field masks, the error subordinate's constant; the named-evidence checker; SV-UVM: the CSR shadow and decode features |The `dtp_xtrig_if` pins inside activity and reset windows, the busy flops, the demux mirrors, the stall counters |The shared AXI-Lite master and `ocah_axi_sva` on the CSR port; polarity-aware pad drivers |`DTP_XTRIG_CHECKER_NEGATIVE`, `DTP_XTRIG_NEGATIVE_CHECK=<n>`
|===

The logs of every family carry the seed and every randomized choice, one
`CHK-<ID> PASS|FAIL expected= observed= context=` line per comparison
where a checker is attached and one `CHECK <name> expected= observed=` line
where the comparison is a plain assertion, and one `CHECKER_SUMMARY` per
finalized checker, so a failure is located from the log alone and the
leaf's recorded seed reproduces it.

===== Fail-closed evidence

A green result is trusted only where the checking can fail. Each checker
family and each no-activity claim has a guard against vacuity and a
positive control:

[cols="1,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Family |Vacuity guard |No-activity evidence and its positive control
|JTAG family and TAP checkers |Finalization rejects zero checks and a missing required ID; `CHK-NONVAC` requires at least one IR and one DR scan per pass |Not a no-activity family
|Shared AXI passive environment |Per-stream minimum compared transactions, `CHK-AXI-NONVAC` (every armed error consumed), `CHK-AXI-CREDITS` (the injection budget spent) |`CHK-AXI-NOACT` and the blocked window `CHK-AXI-BLOCKED`, which fails on any monitored transaction across a gated attempt; the positive control is the counted baseline access of the same bridge before the disable, and on SV-UVM the `CHK-AXI-GATE-*` exact request-count deltas
|Scan windows |Every `CHK-SCAN-WIN` window records that its TCK edge count is non-zero before a quiet or active verdict; a window with no edges fails |A gated port's or SIB's quiet window is paired with the active window of the same signals while enabled in the same scenario
|Cross-trigger windows |`CHK-XTRIG-QUIET` and `CHK-XTRIG-ISOLATION` record the window's cycle count; the route window stays open through the drain tail |The isolation verdict is judged inside the window in which the selected output fired (`CHK-XTRIG-ROUTE-MODEL`); the reset window spans the held cycles and the release
|Debug-disable ledger |`require_cells` refuses to finish with a missing cell; a cell is recorded only after its checker passed; the contradictory tuples are failures, never bins |Every blocked cell has its allowed cell for the same field in the same matrix run
|SV-UVM scoreboard |A required feature with zero comparisons fails; an unpaired expected or observed item fails |Not a no-activity family
|===

===== Bench scope exclusions

Every exclusion names the rows it covers and the fact it rests on; no
blanket exclusion exists. Coverage holes are not exclusions: they are
dispositions in the coverage policy file under the closure policy of
`DTP_FCOV.adoc`.

[cols="1,3,3",options="header",]
|===
|Excluded behavior |Rows |Basis
|Boundary-cell pin behavior |The fifteen section 0 checker rows named in <<dtp-vplan-signoff-jtag-dispositions>>: pin drive, output enables, glitch-free transitions, scan-control timing of the boundary-scan instructions |The BSR is the compact identity loopback of the scan model; the bench has no boundary cells, which belong to the integrating design
|Instrument and downstream TAP behavior |The instrument data of 5.1 to 5.5, the downstream TAP internals of 5.6 to 5.9, and the host scan data path of 5.10 |The instruments are 4-, 5-, and 6-bit loopback stubs, the downstream TAPs are behavioral VIP devices with one writable register, and the host scan-in loops back; the real instruments and TAPs are integration-level
|Disabled elaboration parameters |The disabled-slice path of `ic_reset_slice_chk` and the disabled-instruction paths of every instruction-enable parameter |The bench elaborates one target with every feature enabled; T2 `ast_undefined_selects_bypass` proves the disabled-instruction path
|Unreachable crossbar branches |The demux AW-lock branch of 6.9 and the register-block local stall of 6.7 |Every subordinate behind the crossbar accepts an AW into an empty slot, and the upstream path always presents a ready interface; T9 corroborates the address-map contract
|===

[[dtp-vplan-known-limitations]]
=== Known Limitations

Each row names the level that owns the behaviour the bench cannot reach
(<<dtp-vplan-scope>>); a PASS here closes the reachable clauses of an entry only.

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Limitation |Impact
|iJTAG scan chains end in the bench's 4-, 5-, and 6-bit instrument stubs in `tb_top` |iJTAG scenarios validate the DTP-side SIB plumbing, chain length, and stub capture/update, not an external instrument network (integration level)
|The downstream STAP TAPs are behavioral `ocah_jtag_vip` devices (5-bit IR, IDCODE, one writable TDR) attached only by the STAP-selection scenarios |Other scan scenarios see the STAP host ports as wire loopbacks; the real SMC and SEP debug TAPs are integration level
|The JTAG2AXI bridge ports end in bench responders that map every address |Fabric address decode and the default subordinate are SMC-level; the decode scenarios prove responder-injected DECERR handling on every bridge
|The DUT is elaborated at the `dtp` parameter defaults, and the DFT ports are driven inactive |Entries are closed for that configuration; the SMU's parameterization and DFT-mode behaviour are integration level
|The bring-up startup vector clears all debug disables |Gating behavior is only observable under explicit disable stimulus; the `dbg_disable` group drives directed and seeded disable vectors and closes the 22-cell coverage contract
|SV covergroups are not supported by Verilator |Functional coverage is collected by the `cov/sv/` cover-property modules under Verilator and by covergroups on commercial simulators (see `DTP_FCOV.adoc`)
|===
